Its amplitude is the geometric mean of sheet and residual, and its sign selects swirl or strain. A rank-two silent object has the same source through the same gates but balanced eigenvalues, so the assumption fixes the escape's topology, not its source. The shear fraction is thus a pointwise proxy for cancellation by shear, and a model of the source must be built on what escapes.

</details>


### [58] [Formalising Linear Elliptic PDE Theory in Lean 4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32561)
*Alejandro José Soto Franco,Kobe Marshall-Stevens*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper formalizes in Lean 4 (on Mathlib) the solvability of the Dirichlet problem for second-order linear elliptic operators in divergence form, culminating in classical solvability for regular data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Dirichlet problem for elliptic operators is a cornerstone of PDE theory, but its results had not been fully machine-verified; the authors aim to provide a rigorous, sorry-free Lean 4 formalisation building on Mathlib.

Method: They formalize, without sorry, Poincaré inequality, weak solutions via Lax-Milgram, Rellich-Kondrachov compactness, Fredholm alternative, spectral theorem, interior regularity, and Sobolev embedding, using a self-contained theory of Sobolev spaces developed independently of existing formalisations, linking each prose statement to named Lean declarations.

Result: They obtain a machine-verified formalisation of classical solvability for sufficiently regular coefficients and data, along with all the supporting analytic results listed above.

Conclusion: The work demonstrates that major PDE solvability results can be fully formalized in Lean 4 with no sorry, providing a reusable, independent Sobolev space library and a verified bridge from weak to classical solvability.

Abstract: We formalise in Lean 4, on top of Mathlib, the solvability of the Dirichlet problem for second-order linear elliptic operators in divergence form. The machine-verified results, with no sorry in the development, include the Poincaré inequality, the existence of weak solutions by the Lax-Milgram theorem, Rellich-Kondrachov compactness, the Fredholm alternative, the spectral theorem, interior regularity estimates, and the Sobolev embedding theorem. From these results we obtain a formalisation of classical solvability for sufficiently regular coefficients and data. Our Lean library includes a self-contained theory of Sobolev spaces developed independently of existing formalisations. Throughout the paper we associate each prose statement with the named machine-checked Lean declaration that discharges it.

</details>


### [59] [On solutions for damped fractional Navier-Stokes equations in a critical Lei-Lin-Gevrey space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32583)
*Bruno S. Donato,Wilberclay G. Melo,Thyago S. R. Santos*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Well-posedness, blow-up criteria, Gevrey regularity, and stability for fractional Navier-Stokes equations with cubic damping in Lei-Lin-Gevrey space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To analyze the critical endpoint case γ=1/2 of the fractional Navier-Stokes system with cubic damping, extending well-posedness and regularity theory within the Lei-Lin-Gevrey framework.

Method: Work in the Lei-Lin-Gevrey space X^0_{a,σ}(R^3) with a≥0, σ≥1, treating cubic damping and the critical endpoint γ=1/2.

Result: Establishes local well-posedness and small-data global well-posedness, quantitative blow-up criteria, Gevrey loss-of-radius estimates, and perturbative stability.

Conclusion: The fractional Navier-Stokes equations with cubic damping are well-posed and stable in the critical endpoint case within the specified Lei-Lin-Gevrey space.

Abstract: We study the fractional Navier-Stokes equations with cubic damping in the specific Lei-Lin-Gevrey space $\mathcal{X}^0_{a,σ}(\mathbb{R}^3)$ (with $a\geq0$ and $σ\geq1$). We establish local and small-data global well-posedness, quantitative blow-up criteria, Gevrey loss-of-radius estimates, and perturbative stability in the critical endpoint case $γ=\frac12$ of the usual Navier-Stokes system.

</details>


### [60] [Fitness is asymptotically irrelevant under uniform competition in phenotype-structured populations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32585)
*Artur César Fassoni*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: In phenotype-structured populations with uniform competition, long-term phenotypic distributions converge to the stationary distribution of switching dynamics alone, independent of proliferation rates. This extends compartmental-model results to continuous phenotypes and general Markov semigroup settings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior work showed for compartmental models that long-term phenotype distribution is independent of proliferation rates. This paper seeks to generalize that result to continuous phenotype spaces, examining how switching (advection-diffusion) versus proliferation (fitness-dependent growth) determines asymptotic distributions.

Method: The authors model density u(x,t) via advection-diffusion in phenotype space plus proliferation r(x,t) modulated by g(U). They prove convergence using the property that under uniform competition the reaction term has one sign, making its magnitude an integrable growth rate of the total population, while switching contracts zero-mass perturbations. The approach uses only the Markov semigroup generated by switching.

Result: On a bounded interval with no-flux boundaries, total population converges monotonically to carrying capacity U*, density converges in L1 to U*psi (psi = stationary density of switching), with fitness imprint decaying at the spectral gap. Same convergence holds on the real line under confinement and no-escape conditions, covering Ornstein-Uhlenbeck dynamics, multi-well landscapes, and heavy-tailed densities. Exponential convergence in weighted L2 holds if g'(U*)<0 and psi satisfies a Poincare inequality; no uniform L1 rate for Ornstein-Uhlenbeck dynamics.

Conclusion: Long-term phenotypic distribution under uniform competition is determined solely by switching dynamics, independent of proliferation. The proof generalizes to nonlocal switching by jumps and diffusions with non-gradient drift in bounded R^d domains, where stationary densities are non-Boltzmann and carry circulating flux.

Abstract: We prove that, in phenotype-structured populations under uniform competition, the long-term phenotypic distribution does not depend on the proliferation rates: it is the stationary distribution of the switching dynamics alone. The density u(x,t) of cells with phenotype x evolves by advection and diffusion in phenotype space (switching) and by proliferation at a phenotype-dependent rate r(x,t), modulated by a factor g(U) common to all phenotypes, where U is the total population. This extends to continuous phenotypes a recent result for compartmental models. On a bounded interval with no-flux boundary conditions, the total population converges monotonically to the carrying capacity U*, the density converges in L1 to U*psi, where psi is the stationary density of the switching dynamics, and the nonlinear model is asymptotically equivalent to the linear one; by an explicit estimate, the imprint of fitness decays at the spectral gap of the switching dynamics. On the whole real line we prove the same convergence under two natural conditions, confinement and no escape to infinity, covering Ornstein-Uhlenbeck dynamics, multi-well landscapes, and heavy-tailed densities. If g'(U*) < 0 and psi satisfies a Poincare inequality, convergence is exponential in a weighted L2 norm, while no uniform L1 rate holds for Ornstein-Uhlenbeck dynamics. The proof follows the compartmental one: under uniform competition the reaction term has one sign, so its size is the growth rate of the total population, integrable in time, and the switching dynamics contracts the zero-mass perturbations it produces. Since it uses only the Markov semigroup generated by switching, the proof extends to general mechanisms; we apply it to nonlocal switching by jumps and to diffusions with non-gradient drift in bounded domains of R^d, whose stationary density is not of Boltzmann form and carries a circulating flux.

</details>


### [61] [Sufficiently wide strips uniquely minimize the planar periodic fractional perimeter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32588)
*Juneyoung Seo*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Sufficiently wide periodic strips uniquely minimize the fractional perimeter per period among planar sets with fixed horizontal period and area per period; half-width ≥14 suffices for period one, uniformly in s∈(0,1).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding minimizers of the fractional perimeter under periodicity and area constraints, and determining how wide a strip must be to be the unique minimizer independent of the fractional exponent.

Method: Replace each vertical section by a centered interval of equal length, then prove a quantitative lower bound on perimeter excess in terms of deviation of the half-width profile from its mean. Handle the concavity of strip perimeter in width by showing interaction cost of unequal sections dominates concavity deficit at large mean width; combine small- and large-separation estimates to make the bound s-independent.

Result: Wide strips are the unique minimizers up to vertical translation and null sets. For period one, half-width ≥14 suffices for every s∈(0,1). The argument works for arbitrary nonnegative integrable profiles, including unbounded ones and those vanishing on positive-measure sets.

Conclusion: The fractional perimeter-per-period is minimized uniquely by wide strips, with an s-independent sufficient width, extending to a broad class of measurable competitors.

Abstract: We study the fractional perimeter per period of planar sets with prescribed horizontal period and area per period. We prove that sufficiently wide strips are the unique minimizers among all measurable competitors, up to vertical translation and null sets. For period one, a half-width of at least $14$ suffices for every fractional exponent $s\in(0,1)$. The proof first replaces each vertical section by a centered interval of the same length. We then establish a quantitative lower bound for the perimeter excess in terms of the deviation of the resulting half-width profile from its mean. The main difficulty is that the perimeter of a strip is a concave function of its width. We show that, at large mean width, the interaction cost of unequal sections dominates the corresponding concavity deficit and leaves a positive remainder for every nonconstant profile. Estimates at small and large horizontal separations make the sufficient width bound independent of $s$. The argument applies to arbitrary nonnegative integrable profiles, including unbounded profiles and profiles that vanish on sets of positive measure.

</details>


### [62] [The Direct Scattering Transform for the Intermediate Long Wave Equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32598)
*Peter Perry,Yilun Wu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper rigorously studies the direct scattering problem for the Intermediate Long Wave (ILW) equation, establishing spectral and scattering theory for its Lax operators and paving the way for an inverse scattering transform solution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The direct scattering problem for ILW proposed by Kodama, Ablowitz and Satsuma lacked a rigorous treatment, which is needed to support complete integrability theory and an inverse scattering transform solution of the ILW equation.

Method: The authors derive the Lax operators for ILW and develop their spectral theory, proving finiteness and simplicity of the discrete spectrum, existence and uniqueness of Jost solutions, and constructing the scattering data. They further establish that the Jost solutions satisfy a nonlocal Riemann-Hilbert problem with singularity data given by the scattering data, and show the scattering potential can be reconstructed from the Jost solutions.

Result: Finiteness/simplicity of the discrete spectrum is shown; Jost solutions exist and are unique; scattering data for the Lax operator are constructed; a nonlocal Riemann-Hilbert problem is established and the scattering potential is reconstructible from the Jost solutions.

Conclusion: The work provides a rigorous foundation for the direct scattering theory of ILW and paves the way for solving ILW via the inverse scattering transform.

Abstract: In this paper, we provide a rigorous study of the direct scattering problem proposed by Kodama, Ablowitz and Satsuma for the complete integrability theory of the Intermediate Long Wave (ILW) equation. We derive the Lax operators for ILW, and study their spectral theory. We show finiteness and simplicity of the discrete spectrum, existence and uniqueness of the Jost solutions, and construct the scattering data for the Lax operator. We also show that the Jost solutions satisfy a nonlocal Riemann-Hilbert problem whose singularity data are given by the scattering data, and that the scattering potential can be reconstructed from the Jost solutions. This work paves the way for a solution to ILW via the inverse scattering transform.

</details>


### [63] [Anisotropic quasilinear elliptic systems involving nonlinearities with negative critical terms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32629)
*Elisandra Gloss,Artur Jorge Marinho,Kanishka Perera*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies existence and multiplicity of solutions for a critical growth anisotropic quasilinear elliptic system depending on two parameters λ, μ, coupled via a subcritical perturbation and involving a negative critical part. A scaling parameter γ governs the variational geometry, yielding three multiplicity regimes: γ=1, γ>1, γ<1.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper aims to understand how a critical growth anisotropic quasilinear elliptic system with two parameters, a coupling subcritical term, and a negative critical part affects solution existence and multiplicity, particularly through an appropriate scaling and associated parameter γ.

Method: A specific scaling for the system is identified, introducing a parameter γ that controls the geometry of the variational functional. Multiplicity results are obtained via a recent abstract critical point theorem for symmetric functionals on product spaces when γ≥1, and via a truncation argument when γ<1.

Result: Three distinct types of multiplicity results are established, depending on the regimes γ=1, γ>1, and γ<1.

Conclusion: The multiplicity of solutions depends essentially on the scaling regime governed by γ, with different variational techniques required for γ≥1 versus γ<1.

Abstract: In this paper we investigate existence and multiplicity of solutions for a critical growth anisotropic quasilinear elliptic system, depending on two real parameters $λ,\,μ$, that is coupled through a subcritical perturbation term and involves a negative critical part. We identify a specific scaling for the system and introduce a parameter $γ$ associated with this scaling, which governs the geometry of the corresponding variational functional. We establish three distinct types of multiplicity results, depending on the regimes $γ= 1$, $γ> 1$, and $γ< 1$. The multiple results of multiplicity of solutions are based on a recent abstract critical point theorem for symmetric functionals on product spaces when $γ\geq1$ and on a truncation argument for $γ<1$.

</details>


### [64] [Duality Conditions for Morrey Measures via Parabolic Capacity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32646)
*Lorenzo Braglia*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper gives sharp Morrey-type conditions for a signed Radon measure to belong to the dual of the energy space for nonlinear parabolic p-Laplacian-type equations, and discusses the resulting solution theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize which measures yield well-posed nonlinear parabolic equations with measure data, extending duality/energy space theory to the parabolic p-Laplacian setting.

Method: Use Morrey-type growth conditions on the measure over parabolic cylinders Q_{r,r^p}=B_r(x)×(t-r^p,t+r^p), proving duality under |μ|(Q∩(Ω×(0,T)))≤Mr^{n+p−ϑ} with ϑ<p, and generalizing to cylinders with time length ∼r^q (q>1) with threshold ϑ<min{p,q}; sharpness is shown.

Result: Sufficient Morrey threshold ϑ<p (sharp in general); generalized threshold ϑ<min{p,q} for q>1 cylinders; yields variational, energy, and renormalized solution theories for parabolic equations with measure data.

Conclusion: Sharp Morrey-type duality conditions underpin a unified treatment of nonlinear parabolic equations with measure data, connecting to existing literature on variational, energy, and renormalized solutions.

Abstract: We establish Morrey-type conditions ensuring that a finite signed Radon measure belongs to the dual of the energy space of solutions to nonlinear parabolic equations of $p$-Laplacian type. More precisely, for the parabolic cylinders $Q_{r,r^p}(z):=B_r(x)\times(t-r^p,t+r^p)$, we prove that \[ |μ|\bigl(Q_{r,r^p}(z)\cap(Ω\times (0,T))\bigr) \leq Mr^{n+p-\vartheta}, \qquad \vartheta<p, \] implies duality, and this threshold is sharp in general. More generally, for cylinders with time length proportional to $r^q$, $q>1$, the sufficient threshold is $\vartheta<\min\{p,q\}$. Finally, we discuss the resulting variational, energy, and renormalized solution theories for nonlinear parabolic equations with measure data, relating our conclusions to the existing literature.

</details>


### [65] [Pressure estimates and classifications for steady $H^1_{\rm loc}$-solutions to two-dimensional incompressible Euler equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32647)
*Changfeng Gui,Qinfeng Li,Chunjing Xie,Huan Xu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies 2D steady incompressible Euler flows with finite total curvature, proving rigidity (flows without stagnation points are parallel shear flows), uniform pressure convergence at infinity, and sharp lower bounds on total curvature via pressure oscillation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Finite-total-curvature Euler flows connect to finite-Morse-index solutions of semilinear elliptic equations, motivating a systematic study of their structural and asymptotic properties.

Method: Analysis in the $L^\infty\cap H^1_{\rm loc}$ class, using the pressure as a unifying quantity and standard elliptic estimates; the rigidity argument is extended via the condition $\nabla P\in L^q$ for some $1\le q\le 2$.

Result: In the plane and half-plane, stagnation-free flows are parallel shear flows; pressure converges uniformly to a constant at each end at infinity in the plane, half-plane, and infinite strip; sharp lower bounds for total curvature are obtained in terms of pressure oscillation across several domains.

Conclusion: The rigidity results extend to a broader class and yield a concise new proof of Hamel–Nadirashvili's rigidity theorems with regularity sharpened optimally to $H^1_{\rm loc}$, with pressure serving as the unifying tool.

Abstract: We study two-dimensional steady incompressible Euler flows with finite total curvature. A distinguished family of such flows arises from finite-Morse-index solutions to semilinear elliptic equations in two dimensions. We establish three main properties of finite-total-curvature flows in the $L^\infty\cap H^1_{\rm loc}$ class. First, in the plane and the half-plane, every such flow without stagnation points is a parallel shear flow. Second, in the plane, the half-plane, and the infinite strip, the pressure converges uniformly to a constant at each end at infinity. Third, in the plane, the half-plane, the infinite strip, and the periodic strip, we establish sharp lower bounds for the total curvature in terms of the pressure oscillation.
  The rigidity result in fact extends to a broader class of flows: in the plane and the half-plane, any steady Euler flow without stagnation points is a parallel shear flow provided that $\nabla P\in L^q$ for some $1\le q\le 2$. Combined with standard elliptic estimates for the pressure, this result yields a concise new proof of Hamel and Nadirashvili's rigidity theorems, with the regularity assumption sharpened optimally to $H^1_{\rm loc}$.
  The pressure serves as a unifying quantity throughout the analysis.

</details>


### [66] [Singular analysis of the principal eigenvalue for cooperative elliptic systems with drifts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32693)
*Shuang Liu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper finds the small-diffusion limit of the principal eigenvalue for a 1D cooperative elliptic system with component-dependent drifts and Neumann boundary conditions, showing that unlike scalar equations the limit is not purely local but governed by an Aubry set that can include intervals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: For scalar equations, the small-diffusion limit of the principal eigenvalue is determined by local data at drift equilibria and boundary points. The authors investigate whether such localization still holds for cooperative systems, where switching between components may change the picture.

Method: They prove that logarithmic transforms of all component eigenfunctions converge to a common solution of a scalar Hamilton-Jacobi equation. The associated Aubry set controls the eigenvalue asymptotics; under nondegeneracy it decomposes into isolated points, regular intervals, and composite intervals containing common drift zeros. Each class's effective value is computed via local Ornstein-Uhlenbeck spectra and interval transport problems.

Result: Localization fails for systems: switching between components can make entire intervals spectrally relevant. The limit of the principal eigenvalue equals the minimum over the class values, and a unique minimizing class determines the common logarithmic eigenfunction profile.

Conclusion: The small-diffusion asymptotics of cooperative systems are governed by a Hamilton-Jacobi/Aubry set structure yielding a minimum-of-class-values formula, generalizing the purely local scalar picture to include interval-based spectral contributions.

Abstract: We determine the small-diffusion limit of the principal eigenvalue for a one-dimensional cooperative elliptic system with component-dependent drifts and Neumann boundary conditions. For scalar equations, this limit is determined by local data at the drift equilibria and boundary points. We show that such localization fails for systems: switching between components can make some intervals spectrally relevant. We first prove that the logarithmic transforms of all component eigenfunctions converge to the same function, which solves a scalar Hamilton-Jacobi equation. The associated Aubry set determines the asymptotic behavior of the principal eigenvalue. Under nondegeneracy assumptions, this set decomposes into isolated points, regular intervals, and composite intervals containing common drift zeros. We then identify the effective value of each class through local Ornstein-Uhlenbeck spectra and interval transport problems, and establish that the limit of the principal eigenvalue is the minimum of these class values. A unique minimizing class also determines the common logarithmic eigenfunction profile.

</details>


### [67] [Blow-Up of Modified Navier--Stokes Equations in Nonhomogeneous Fourier Spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32719)
*Jamel Benameur,Lotfi Jlali*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper analyzes 3D incompressible Navier-Stokes equations with componentwise nonlinear damping in Fourier and Fourier-Gevrey spaces based on X^0(R^3), establishing local existence, blow-up criteria, and lower bounds near finite maximal existence time.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the behavior of solutions to the damped 3D Navier-Stokes equations, particularly their regularity, blow-up criteria, and quantitative behavior near potential finite-time singularity, using analytic (Fourier-Gevrey) techniques.

Method: Works in Fourier and Fourier-Gevrey spaces based on X^0(R^3); establishes local-in-time existence/uniqueness and maximal solutions; derives blow-up criteria including an integral criterion in a weaker exponential weight; obtains a quantitative lower bound near the maximal existence time; iterates the loss of exponential weight to extend the lower bound to the unweighted Fourier space.

Result: Local-in-time existence and uniqueness of solutions and unique maximal solutions; blow-up criteria in Fourier-Gevrey framework including an integral blow-up criterion in weaker exponential weight; a quantitative lower bound near a possible finite maximal existence time; and a corresponding lower bound in unweighted Fourier space X^0(R^3).

Conclusion: The damped 3D Navier-Stokes system admits unique maximal solutions with characterizable blow-up and quantifiable lower bounds near finite maximal time, valid even in the unweighted Fourier space via iterated loss of exponential weight.

Abstract: We study the three-dimensional incompressible Navier--Stokes equations with a componentwise nonlinear damping term of the form $α\sum_{k=1}^3 u_k^{2m+1}e_k$ in Fourier and Fourier-- Gevrey spaces based on $\mathcal{X}^0(\mathbb{R}^3)$. We first establish local-in-time existence and uniqueness of solutions and obtain the corresponding unique maximal solutions. We then derive blow-up criteria in the Fourier--Gevrey framework, including an integral blow-up criterion in a weaker exponential weight. Moreover, we establish a quantitative lower bound for the solution near a possible finite maximal existence time. By iterating the loss of exponential weight, we finally obtain a corresponding lower bound in the unweighted Fourier space $\mathcal{X}^0(\mathbb{R}^3)$.

</details>


### [68] [Synchronized Solutions for Liouville Systems with Sign-Changing Coefficients](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32812)
*Woongbae Park,Lei Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes existence of synchronized solutions for singular Liouville systems on closed Riemann surfaces with a sign-changing common coefficient, via reduction to a scalar mean-field equation along an intrinsic ray in parameter space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Direct continuation from positive coefficients fails because degenerate zeroes inevitably appear along such a homotopy. The authors seek an alternative route to existence for sign-changing coefficients.

Method: They identify an intrinsic ray in parameter space where the system reduces exactly to a scalar sign-changing mean-field equation, then combine the variational existence theory of De Marchis–López-Soriano–Ruiz with Wu's nodal singularity compactness theorem.

Result: Existence is obtained for every noncritical parameter on the ray, allowing positive singular sources on the nodal curve. When the surface has nonpositive Euler characteristic and each negative component of the coefficient is a disk, the topological hypothesis of the scalar min–max construction holds automatically.

Conclusion: The reduction-plus-compactness approach yields existence results for singular Liouville systems with sign-changing coefficients, overcoming the obstruction faced by direct continuation methods.

Abstract: We consider singular Liouville systems on closed Riemann surfaces with a common sign-changing coefficient. Direct continuation from a positive coefficient is obstructed by the unavoidable appearance of degenerate zeroes along such a homotopy. We instead identify an intrinsic ray in the parameter space on which the system admits synchronized solutions and reduces exactly to a scalar sign-changing mean-field equation. Combining this reduction with the variational existence theory of De Marchis--López-Soriano--Ruiz and the nodal singularity compactness theorem of Wu, we obtain existence for every noncritical parameter on this ray. Positive singular sources are allowed on the nodal curve. In particular, when the surface has nonpositive Euler characteristic and every negative component of the coefficient is a disk, the topological hypothesis required by the scalar min--max construction is automatic.

</details>


### [69] [Second order estimates on transition layers for the Allen-Cahn equation with homogeneous Neumann boundary conditions: Part I - Boundary-orthogonal transition layers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32829)
*Akashdeep Dey,Wenkui Du,Davide Parise,Lorenzo Sarnataro*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes uniform second-order Hölder estimates and quantitative mean curvature decay for nodal hypersurfaces of stable Allen-Cahn solutions with Neumann boundary conditions, up to the boundary, in dimensions ≤10.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Transition layers meeting the boundary orthogonally are geometrically subtle; existing interior theory (Wang-Wei) does not handle boundary effects on the nodal set's mean curvature.

Method: Adapts the Wang-Wei technique, adding a correction term determined by the geometry of the ambient boundary. The leading correction cancels on the nodal set but survives on non-zero level sets, preventing uniform mean curvature decay there.

Result: Under an a priori bound on the enhanced second fundamental form in the transition region, uniform second-order Hölder estimates and quantitative mean curvature decay for nodal hypersurfaces are obtained up to the boundary, for ambient dimensions ≤10.

Conclusion: The boundary induces a phenomenon with no interior analogue: mean curvature of non-zero level sets need not decay uniformly, as exhibited by Kowalczyk's stable solutions.

Abstract: We study stable solutions of the Allen-Cahn equation with homogeneous Neumann boundary condition on Riemannian manifolds with boundary in the regime where the transition layers meet the boundary orthogonally. Under an a priori bound for the enhanced second fundamental form in the transition region, we establish uniform second-order Hölder estimates and quantitative mean curvature decay for the nodal hypersurfaces up to the boundary in ambient dimensions at most 10. The proof adapts the technique pioneered by Wang-Wei, adding a further correction determined by the geometry of the ambient boundary. Its leading contribution to mean curvature cancels on the nodal set but survives on non-zero level sets in the transition region, whose mean curvature need not decay uniformly. This phenomenon has no interior analogue and is exhibited by stable solutions constructed by Kowalczyk.

</details>


### [70] [Global solutions for non-isothermal magnetoviscoelastic fluids in the absence of deformation diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32853)
*Yuanzhen Shao,Gieri Simonett*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies a non-isothermal incompressible magnetoviscoelastic fluid model in R^N (N=2,3), coupling Navier-Stokes with evolution equations for deformation tensor, temperature, and magnetization, with temperature-dependent coefficients and no artificial diffusion on the deformation tensor.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Magnetoviscoelastic fluids involve coupled hyperbolic-parabolic dynamics that are challenging to analyze, especially without artificial diffusion on the deformation tensor and with temperature-dependent material coefficients. Understanding well-posedness underpins the physical validity of such models.

Method: The model couples incompressible Navier-Stokes equations with evolution equations for the deformation tensor, temperature, and magnetization. The analysis introduces an auxiliary variable combining velocity and the inverse deformation tensor, which uncovers a hidden dissipative structure and enables uniform higher-order energy estimates.

Result: Local well-posedness of strong solutions is established for sufficiently regular initial data, and global existence is proved for small perturbations of a constant equilibrium.

Conclusion: The hidden dissipative structure in the coupled system is key to obtaining global existence for small data, providing a robust framework for analyzing non-isothermal magnetoviscoelastic fluids.

Abstract: We study a non-isothermal model for incompressible magnetoviscoelastic fluids in $\mathbb{R}^N$, $N=2,3$, coupling the incompressible Navier-Stokes equations with the evolution equations for the deformation tensor, temperature, and magnetization. The material coefficients are allowed to depend on the temperature, and no artificial diffusion is imposed on the deformation tensor, leading to a coupled hyperbolic-parabolic system. We establish local well-posedness of strong solutions for sufficiently regular initial data and prove global existence for small perturbations of a constant equilibrium. The global analysis relies on a suitable auxiliary variable combining the velocity and the inverse deformation tensor, which reveals a hidden dissipative structure and allows us to derive uniform higher-order energy estimates for the coupled system.

</details>


### [71] [Formalization of the Galerkin Construction for the Two-Dimensional Navier--Stokes Equations in Lean](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33033)
*Weinan Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper formalizes in Lean 4 the Galerkin construction of global Leray-Hopf weak solutions for 2D Navier-Stokes on bounded domains with no-slip conditions, plus finite-horizon forced solutions on rectangles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Provide a rigorous, machine-checked formalization of classical existence results for 2D Navier-Stokes weak solutions, bridging PDE analysis and interactive theorem proving.

Method: Lean 4 formalization using Galerkin approximation, including divergence-free graph spaces, compact spectral coordinates, transport cancellation, Ladyzhenskaya estimates, and simultaneous space-time compactness; abstract Hilbert-space framework with compact energy-to-velocity embedding and skew trilinear nonlinearity.

Result: For any solenoidal L^2 initial datum, solutions have weakly continuous velocity paths, satisfy the weak equation locally in time, and obey the energy inequality at every time; forced finite-horizon solutions formalized for rectangles.

Conclusion: The work delivers a fully formalized Galerkin existence theory and generalizes it to abstract evolution equations with compact embedding and skew nonlinearity.

Abstract: We formalize in Lean 4 the Galerkin construction of global Leray--Hopf weak solutions for the unforced two-dimensional Navier--Stokes equations on arbitrary bounded open domains with no-slip boundary conditions. For every initial datum in the solenoidal \(L^2\) velocity space, the solution has a weakly continuous velocity path, satisfies the weak equation locally in time, and obeys the energy inequality at every time. For rectangles, we also formalize finite-horizon solutions with continuous forcing in the dual energy space. The development includes divergence-free graph spaces, compact spectral coordinates, transport cancellation, Ladyzhenskaya estimates, and simultaneous space--time compactness. The abstract Hilbert-space results apply to evolution equations with a compact energy-to-velocity embedding and a skew trilinear nonlinearity.

</details>


### [72] [Symmetry of solutions to nonlocal Neumann problems with the fractional Laplacian in half spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33103)
*Baiyu Liu,Wenli Tan*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies fractional Laplacian equations in half-spaces with nonlocal Neumann boundary conditions and proves symmetry and monotonicity of decaying solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Symmetry results for fractional Laplacian problems with nonlocal Neumann conditions were previously unexplored, so this work aims to fill that gap.

Method: Establishes a decay at infinity principle and a narrow region principle, then applies them with the direct method of moving planes.

Result: Derives symmetry and monotonicity properties for decaying solutions to both single equations and systems.

Conclusion: These are the first symmetry results for fractional Laplacian equations with nonlocal Neumann conditions.

Abstract: This paper investigates the fractional Laplacian equation in half-spaces, focusing on cases with nonlocal Neumann boundary conditions. We consider both single equations and systems, and establish two key principles: the decay at infinity principle and the narrow region principle. These foundational results, combined with the direct method of moving planes, allow us to derive symmetry and monotonicity properties for decaying solutions. To the best of our knowledge, these are the first results concerning the symmetry of solutions to fractional Laplacian equations with nonlocal Neumann conditions.

</details>


### [73] [Global well-posedness and Decay estimates for solutions of the Navier--Stokes--Maxwell system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33241)
*Belkacem Said-Houari*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Global well-posedness and optimal decay rates are established for small strong solutions of the 2D/3D Navier-Stokes-Maxwell system, with the electric field decaying faster than velocity and magnetic components.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Navier-Stokes-Maxwell system couples a dissipative parabolic fluid with a hyperbolic electromagnetic field via the Lorenz force and Ohm's law. This parabolic-hyperbolic structure makes global solvability and sharp decay analysis challenging, especially in 2D where borderline decay leads to logarithmic losses.

Method: Detailed study of the linearized system to extract refined decay of electromagnetic components; time-weighted energy method capturing distinct dissipative properties; a compensating functional exploiting electric-magnetic coupling to recover missing magnetic dissipation; careful treatment of nonlinear terms.

Result: For sufficiently small initial data with suitable Sobolev regularity, global strong solutions exist and exhibit optimal decay rates for the solution and its higher-order derivatives. The electric field decays with an extra factor (1+t)^{-1/2} relative to velocity and magnetic components.

Conclusion: The parabolic-hyperbolic coupling of the Navier-Stokes-Maxwell system admits global small-data solutions with optimal decay, and the compensartory functional approach effectively overcomes the 2D logarithmic loss and the magnetic field's lack of dissipation.

Abstract: We investigate the global solvability and the large-time asymptotic behavior of solutions to the Navier--Stokes--Maxwell system in two and three space dimensions. The system describes the interaction between a viscous incompressible fluid and an electromagnetic field through the Lorenz force and Ohm's law. The system exhibits a parabolic--hyperbolic coupling in which the dissipative Navier--Stokes system interacts with the hyperbolic Maxwell system. The Maxwell system is damped through Ohm's law with the Maxwell correction. For sufficiently small initial data with suitable Sobolev regularity, we establish the global well-posedness of strong solutions and derive optimal decay rates for the solution and its higher-order spatial derivatives. In addition, we show that the electric field decays faster by the extra factor $(1+t)^{-1/2}$ compared to the velocity and magnetic components.
  One of the key ingredients of our analysis is a detailed study of the linearized system, which reveals refined decay properties of the electromagnetic components. These linear estimates play a crucial role in the analysis of the two-dimensional case, allowing us to overcome the logarithmic loss arising from the borderline decay estimates.
  For the nonlinear system, our approach is based on a time-weighted energy method, specifically designed to capture the distinct dissipative properties of the fluid and electromagnetic components. A suitable compensating functional is introduced to exploit the coupling between the electric and magnetic fields and thereby recover the missing dissipation of the magnetic field. Combined with careful analysis of the nonlinear terms, these estimates yield our desired result.

</details>


### [74] [Forward self-similar solutions of the Navier-Stokes equations in R^n](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33316)
*Baishun Lai,Changxing Miao,Xiaoxin Zheng*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies forward self-similar solutions of the incompressible Navier-Stokes equations with homogeneous initial data σ(x/|x|)/|x|, focusing on dimensions 3≤n≤5, with special attention to the critical 4D case, establishing existence, regularity, and sharp spatial decay.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Forward self-similar solutions with singular homogeneous data are important for understanding possible singular behavior of the Navier-Stokes equations, especially in the critical four-dimensional setting where new linear and nonlinear estimates are needed.

Method: A Galerkin scheme is developed for the Leray profile problem with a decomposition of self-similar data and nonlocal heat kernel effects. Coercivity of the Leray operator gives uniform a priori estimates. In 4D, new Stokes-type estimates for the linear Leray system are proved via an integral/singular integral representation and Calderón-Zygmund L^p boundedness, combined with a compactness estimate for the convective term. Decay is treated in weighted spaces using frequency-localized estimates.

Result: Weak forward self-similar solutions are obtained for 3≤n≤5. In 4D, regularity is closed via the Stokes-type estimates and convective compactness. For decay, the non-integrability of the low-frequency kernel causes a logarithmic loss under σ∈W^{1,∞}(S^3); assuming σ∈C^{1,γ}(S^3), 0<γ<1, frequency-localized estimates remove this loss and yield sharp decay.

Conclusion: The work establishes existence and sharp spatial decay of forward self-similar Navier-Stokes solutions with homogeneous data, highlighting the special role of dimension four and the interplay between kernel non-integrability, data regularity, and decay rates.

Abstract: We investigate forward self-similar solutions to the incompressible Navier--Stokes equations with homogeneous initial data $u_0(x)=σ(x/|x|)/|x|$, $x\in\mathbb R^n$, focusing on the critical four-dimensional case. For the existence theory in dimensions $3\leq n\leq5$, we develop a Galerkin scheme for the Leray profile problem, featuring a decomposition of the self-similar data and nonlocal heat kernel effects across spatial scales. Exploiting the structural properties of the data and the coercivity of the Leray operator, we derive uniform \textit{a priori} estimates, pass to the limit, and obtain weak forward self-similar solutions. In the 4D case, we prove new Stokes-type estimates for the linear Leray system. The inverse Leray operator admits an integral representation as a singular integral operator; its $L^p$-boundedness follows from Calderón--Zygmund theory. Combined with a 4D compactness estimate for the convective term, this closes the regularity iteration. For spatial decay, we reformulate the problem in weighted spaces. The non-integrability of the low-frequency kernel causes a logarithmic loss under $σ\in W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb S^3)$. With $σ\in C^{1,γ}(\mathbb S^3)$, $0<γ<1$, frequency-localized estimates remove this loss and yield sharp decay.

</details>


### [75] [Well-posedness of Boussinesq Equation with Linear Restoring Force](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33358)
*Amin Esfahani,Nazerke Zhardemova*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: First local well-posedness theory for the n-dimensional Boussinesq equation with a linear restoring force, using modified Sobolev and Bourgain-type spaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Boussinesq equation with a linear restoring force has a modified dispersion relation, and no well-posedness results existed for this model. Standard Sobolev/Bourgain spaces do not match the new dispersion, so adapted function spaces were needed.

Method: Introduce modified Sobolev spaces H_ω^s and Bourgain-type spaces X_ω^{s,b} adapted to the modified dispersion relation. The restoring force yields a new phase function with different low-frequency behavior than the classical Boussinesq equation, altering the energy functional. Prove bilinear estimates for quadratic nonlinearities and trilinear estimates for cubic nonlinearities.

Result: Local well-posedness established under suitable conditions on s and ω, implied by the bilinear and trilinear estimates.

Conclusion: This is the first well-posedness result for this model.

Abstract: We establish a local well-posedness theory for the $n-$dimensional Boussinesq equation with a linear restoring force in a modified Sobolev spaces $H_ω^s$ and Bourgain-type spaces $X_ω^{s,b}$, which are adapted to its modified dispersion relation. The restoring force introduces a new phase function whose behavior in the low-frequencies is different from ones of the classical Boussinesq equation, that in turn, affects the associated energy functional. We prove bilinear and trilinear estimates for quadratic and cubic nonlinearities, respectively, which in turn imply local well-posedness under suitable conditions on $s$ and $ω$. To our knowledge, this is the first well-posedness result for this model.

</details>


### [76] [Scattering of group-invariant solutions below the ground state for the fourth-order NLS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33381)
*Koichi Komada*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Extends scattering results for the focusing L^2-supercritical, H^2-dot-subcritical nonlinear fourth-order Schrödinger equation from radially symmetric to group-invariant solutions, removing a prior hypothesis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Scattering below the ground state was known for radially symmetric solutions (Guo 2016; Dinh 2021) and for group-invariant solutions only under an extra hypothesis (Komada-Masaki 2024). This paper aims to remove that hypothesis.

Method: Establish a non-optimal scattering result for general solutions, where the action threshold is less than a certain fraction of the ground state's action, analogous to Pausader-Shao (2010).

Result: Scattering results are extended to group-invariant solutions without requiring the previous hypothesis.

Conclusion: The hypothesis in Komada-Masaki is removed, yielding scattering for group-invariant solutions via an action-threshold argument.

Abstract: We consider the focusing, $L^2$-supercritical and $\dot{H}^2$-subcritical nonlinear fourth-order Schrödinger equation. The scattering of radially symmetric solutions below the ground state was proved by Guo [Comm. Partial Differential Equations (2016)] and Dinh [Nonlinearity (2021)]. In this paper, we extend the scattering results to group-invariant solutions. In Komada--Masaki [Nonlinearity (2024)], the scattering of group-invariant solutions below the ground state was proved under a certain hypothesis. To remove the hypothesis, we establish the non-optimal scattering result for general solutions, where the threshold of action is less than certain fraction of the action of the ground state. This result is analogous to that in Pausader--Shao [J. Hyperbolic Differ. Equ. (2010)] for the $L^2$-critical nonlinear fourth-order Schrödinger equation.

</details>


### [77] [Simultaneous recovery of various time-dependent coefficients in a hyperbolic equation with space-time nonlocal attenuation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33393)
*Yavar Kian,Gunther Uhlmann*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper solves inverse coefficient problems for a hyperbolic equation with space-time nonlocal attenuation using a local source-to-solution map, uniquely determining leading and lower order coefficients depending on both space and time, with data collected on disjoint sets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Many inverse coefficient problems remain open or fundamentally intractable for linear and nonlinear hyperbolic equations without attenuation. The authors aim to exploit nonlocal attenuation to overcome these limitations.

Method: They leverage interactions induced by nonlocal attenuation (memory effects, unique continuation principle), integrate the interplay between local and nonlocal operators, and use structural properties of hyperbolic equations. Data is collected on disjoint sets in both space and time.

Result: They simultaneously determine general classes of leading and lower order coefficients depending on both space and time, extending existing theory and introducing the notion of data on sets disjoint in both space and time for hyperbolic equations.

Conclusion: Nonlocal attenuation enables unique determination of coefficients that would otherwise be intractable, providing a novel framework for inverse problems with disjoint space-time data.

Abstract: This article is devoted to the inverse problem of uniquely determining different classes of coefficients in a hyperbolic equation with space-time nonlocal attenuation from a local source-to-solution map. Our objective is to exploit the nonlocal attenuation mechanism to address inverse coefficient problems that remain open, or are even fundamentally intractable, for linear and nonlinear hyperbolic equations without attenuation. More precisely, by leveraging the interactions induced by nonlocal attenuation, we simultaneously determine general classes of leading and lower order coefficients depending on both space and time. In addition, we formulate our results using data collected on disjoint sets, thereby extending the existing theory and introducing the notion of data on sets disjoint in both space and time for hyperbolic equations. Our approach builds on key features of nonlocal operators, such as memory effects and the unique continuation principle, while integrating the interplay between local and nonlocal operators along with structural properties of hyperbolic equations.

</details>


### [78] [An entropy-level method for quantitative time-asymptotic in the Fokker-Planck model for fermions -- appearance of non saturation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33396)
*R. Alonso,B. Lods*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes quantitative time-asymptotic thermalisation for the Fermi-Dirac-Fokker-Planck equation for general, including arbitrarily cold, initial data by combining stability, entropy-based convergence, and a De Giorgi level-set iteration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Fermi-Dirac-Fokker-Planck equation corrects the classical Fokker-Planck model to include the Pauli exclusion principle, and understanding its long-time thermalisation behavior is important. Existing entropy methods typically give convergence only for a dense set of initial data, leaving general (especially cold) initial data unaddressed.

Method: A novel approach combining three ingredients: (1) stability of solutions, (2) quantitative convergence for a dense set of initial data via a robust entropy method, and (3) a De Giorgi level-set iteration to extend results to general initial data.

Result: Quantitative time-asymptotic thermalisation is proved for general initial data, in particular quantitative convergence for arbitrarily cold initial data.

Conclusion: The combined stability-entropy-De Giorgi framework yields rigorous quantitative thermalisation for the Fermi-Dirac-Fokker-Planck equation, extending convergence beyond dense initial data to general and cold initial data.

Abstract: In this contribution we consider the Fokker-Planck equation for fermions, commonly known as the Fermi-Dirac-Fokker-Planck equation. This equation is a correction of the classical Fokker-Planck model that incorporates the Pauli exclusion principle. We present a novel approach that combines three ingredients: (1) stability of solutions, (2) quantitative convergence for a dense set of initial data via a robust entropy method, and (3) a De Giorgi level-set iteration, to establish quantitative time-asymptotic thermalisation for general initial data. In particular, we prove quantitative convergence for arbitrarily cold initial data.

</details>


### [79] [Regularity for vectorial double phase functionals with two modulating coefficients and logarithmic-type growth](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33479)
*Yumi Kim,Jehan Oh*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves local Hölder continuity of vector-valued minimizers for double phase functionals with logarithmic-type growth and two modulating coefficients, establishing gradient Hölder continuity when both coefficients are Hölder continuous.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Double phase functionals with logarithmic-type growth (energy density a(x)|Du|^p + b(x)|Du|^p log(e+|Du|)) generalize classical double phase and logarithmic models. Understanding regularity of minimizers when coefficients may vanish but their sum stays bounded away from zero is important for these nonuniformly elliptic problems.

Method: Assumes a(·) uniformly continuous and b(·) satisfies a vanishing log-Hölder continuity condition. Proof combines Gehring-type higher integrability with a comparison argument.

Result: Minimizers are locally Hölder continuous for every exponent in (0,1). If both coefficients are Hölder continuous, then the gradient of a minimizer is locally Hölder continuous.

Conclusion: The paper establishes full local Hölder regularity (and gradient Hölder regularity under stronger coefficient assumptions) for vector-valued minimizers of double phase functionals with logarithmic growth, extending regularity theory to this setting with two modulating coefficients.

Abstract: We study vector-valued local minimizers of double phase functionals with two modulating coefficients and logarithmic-type growth. The energy density is $a(x)|Du|^p+b(x)|Du|^p\log(e+|Du|)$, where the nonnegative coefficients $a(\cdot)$ and $b(\cdot)$ may vanish but their sum is bounded away from zero. Assuming that $a(\cdot)$ is uniformly continuous and that $b(\cdot)$ satisfies a vanishing log-Hölder continuity condition, we prove that minimizers are locally Hölder continuous for every exponent in $(0,1)$. If both coefficients are Hölder continuous, then the gradient of a minimizer is locally Hölder continuous. The proof combines Gehring-type higher integrability with a comparison argument.

</details>


### [80] [Boundary pointwise regularity for the $p$-Laplace equation on $C^{1,\mathrm{Dini}}$ domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33577)
*Xuemei Li,Yuanyuan Lian*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes boundary pointwise regularity for p-Laplace equation solutions, treating curved boundaries as perturbations of flat ones, obtaining Lipschitz regularity, the Hopf lemma, and C^1 regularity under C^{1,Dini} conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding boundary pointwise regularity of p-Laplace equation solutions, particularly for curved domains, motivates this study, extending known flat-boundary results.

Method: The authors treat curved boundaries as perturbations of flat ones, use the exterior C^{1,Dini} condition for boundary pointwise Lipschitz regularity, the interior C^{1,Dini} condition for the Hopf lemma, and a compactness argument for boundary pointwise C^1 regularity under the C^{1,Dini} condition.

Result: Boundary pointwise Lipschitz regularity is established under the exterior C^{1,Dini} condition, the Hopf lemma under the interior C^{1,Dini} condition, and boundary pointwise C^1 regularity under the C^{1,Dini} condition.

Conclusion: The p-Laplace equation admits boundary pointwise Lipschitz, Hopf lemma, and C^1 regularity under appropriate C^{1,Dini} boundary conditions, via perturbation and compactness techniques.

Abstract: This paper investigates the boundary pointwise regularity of solutions to the p-Laplace equation. By treating curved boundaries as perturbations of flat ones, we establish the boundary pointwise Lipschitz regularity under the exterior $C^{1,\mathrm{Dini}}$ condition and the Hopf lemma under the interior $C^{1,\mathrm{Dini}}$ condition. We also establish the boundary pointwise $C^1$ regularity under the $C^{1,\mathrm{Dini}}$ condition by a compactness argument.

</details>


### [81] [Lamb dipole stability without sign condition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33578)
*Ken Abe,Kyudong Choi,In-Jee Jeong,Guolin Qin*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves Lyapunov stability of the Lamb dipole for 2D incompressible Euler equations on the half-plane, without assuming a sign condition on vorticity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing dipole stability relies on conserved weighted norms valid only for single-signed vorticity. For mixed-sign vorticities, the x2-weighted norm grows, creating a key obstacle.

Method: A Lagrangian bootstrap argument: the solution is carefully split into several pieces whose mutual interactions are analyzed, with the most delicate case being vorticity drifting to infinity vertically.

Result: Lyapunov stability of the Lamb dipole is established for mixed-sign vorticity, overcoming the growth of the weighted norm.

Conclusion: The strategy offers a starting point for stability analysis of more general vortex configurations, such as multiple dipoles and vortex rings with mixed-sign vorticity.

Abstract: For the 2D incompressible Euler equations on the half-plane, we establish Lyapunov stability of the Lamb dipole without sign condition on the vorticity. The main challenge arises from growth of the $x_{2}$ weighted norm for mixed sign vorticities, whereas this norm is conserved and responsible for dipole stability in the single signed vorticity case. This difficulty is handled by a Lagrangian bootstrap argument, based on carefully dividing the solution into several pieces and analyzing interactions among them, with the most delicate interaction involving vorticity drifting to infinity in the vertical direction. We expect that the strategy developed here will provide a starting point for the stability analysis of more general vortex configurations, including interactions among multiple vortex dipoles and among multiple vortex rings in settings involving mixed-sign vorticity.

</details>


### [82] [The $m$-Laplace equation with a gradient term II: classification in the second critical case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33583)
*Tian Wu,Jin Yan,Hua Zhu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper classifies all positive locally bounded weak solutions of the equation -Δ_m u = u^p|Du|^q in R^n for a specific critical exponent p, showing each solution is either constant or in an explicit radial family, with no global bound or decay assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Classifying entire positive solutions of quasilinear equations like -Δ_m u = u^p|Du|^q is a central problem in nonlinear analysis, but standard methods often require global boundedness, decay, or finite-energy assumptions to rule out pathologies. The authors aim to remove such assumptions and work in the weak solution class, where regularity at critical points is delicate.

Method: The proof rests on a scalar differential identity plus a maximum principle that holds even at critical points. These yield a sharp gradient bound, a classification of normalized solutions, and a first-contact (sliding/translation) argument extending the result to arbitrary entire solutions. Regularity and behavior at critical points are handled within the weak solution class; global C^2 regularity is not assumed.

Result: For 1<m<n and 0<q<m-1, with the critical exponent p = ((m-q)/(n-m))(n + q/(m-1-q)) - 1, every positive locally bounded weak solution of -Δ_m u = u^p|Du|^q in R^n is either constant or, up to translation and scaling, an explicit radial solution. No global bound, decay, or finite-energy assumption is needed.

Conclusion: The paper gives a complete classification of positive locally bounded weak solutions at a specific critical exponent, removing usual analytic assumptions. The radial solutions identified are the only nonconstant possibilities. The techniques-local scalar identities, critical-point-valid maximum principle, sharp gradient bounds, and first-contact arguments-may extend to related quasilinear problems involving gradient-dependent nonlinearities.

Abstract: Let $1<m<n$ and $0<q<m-1$. We classify positive, locally bounded weak solutions of \[-Δ_m u=u^p|Du|^q\quad\text{in }\mathbb R^n,\qquad p=\frac{m-q}{n-m}\left(n+\frac{q}{m-1-q}\right)-1.\] Every solution is constant or belongs to an explicit family of radial solutions, up to translation and scaling. No global bound, decay, or finite-energy assumption is required. The proof is based on a scalar differential identity and a maximum principle that remains valid at critical points. These lead to a sharp gradient bound, classification of normalized solutions, and a first-contact argument for arbitrary entire solutions. Local regularity and the behavior at critical points are treated throughout in the weak solution class; global $C^2$ regularity is not assumed.

</details>


### [83] [Scattering for Short-Range Mass-Subcritical Choquard Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33775)
*Alessandro Michelangeli,Mirko Tarulli*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Scattering for the defocusing inhomogeneous generalized Choquard equation with conformal energy-space data is proved in the full short-range mass-subcritical regime, including scattering in L², H¹, and (homogeneous case) Σ above the Strauss threshold.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior work on Choquard-type equations often relied on intermediate Strichartz-Sobolev estimates that impose extra algebraic restrictions on parameters, limiting the range of covered regimes.

Method: A key nonlinear-term estimate yields L² scattering in the short-range mass-subcritical regime; a pseudoconformal argument in compactified variables upgrades this to H¹ scattering; in the homogeneous case, Galilean vector-field estimates give Σ scattering above the Strauss threshold.

Result: Scattering is established in L² and H¹ for the full short-range mass-subcritical regime, and in Σ above the Strauss threshold in the homogeneous case, without extra algebraic restrictions.

Conclusion: The approach removes algebraic constraints from intermediate Strichartz-Sobolev estimates, providing a cleaner route to scattering for these Choquard equations.

Abstract: We study scattering for the defocusing inhomogeneous generalized Choquard equation with data in the conformal energy space. In the full short-range mass-subcritical regime, a key estimate for the nonlinear term yields scattering in \(L^2\). A pseudoconformal argument in the compactified variables then gives scattering in \(H^1\). In the homogeneous case, estimates based on the Galilean vector field further imply scattering in \(Σ\) above the Strauss threshold. The method avoids the additional algebraic restrictions arising from intermediate Strichartz-Sobolev estimates.

</details>


### [84] [Maximal Regularity and Existence for Superquadratic Parabolic Hamilton--Jacobi Equations: The Endpoint case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33798)
*Fanze Kong,Xiaoyu Zeng*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves interior maximal $L^{q_c}$-regularity for bounded strong solutions of a nonlinear parabolic equation with gradient nonlinearity $|Du|^\gamma$, using a two-scale blow-up argument to handle critical gradient energy concentration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The main difficulty is the possible concentration of the critical gradient energy, which obstructs uniform estimates in the critical space $L^{q_c}$. Uniform regularity is needed to handle families of solutions whose source terms are only equi-integrable, not bounded in a stronger space.

Method: A two-scale blow-up argument: the first scale produces a small Hamiltonian coefficient; energy normalization at the second scale yields strong endpoint compactness; a parabolic Liouville theorem rules out concentration. A uniform little-Hölder estimate plus critical Gagliardo–Nirenberg interpolation allows absorption of the nonlinear term in the parabolic Calderón–Zygmund estimate.

Result: Interior maximal $L^{q_c}$-regularity estimates, uniform over uniformly bounded families with source terms in a bounded, uniformly equi-integrable subset of $L^{q_c}$. Also, existence of bounded strong solutions for $f\in L^{q_c}$ with continuous initial data, and subsequential strong interior convergence of smooth approximations in $W^{2,1}_{q_c}$.

Conclusion: The two-scale blow-up approach successfully controls critical gradient energy concentration, yielding sharp uniform regularity and strong convergence results for the nonlinear parabolic problem.

Abstract: We establish interior maximal $L^{q_c}$-regularity for bounded strong solutions of $u_t-Δu+|Du|^γ=f$ in $\mathbb{T}^d\times(0,T)$, where $d\geq 2$, $γ>2$, and $q_c=(d+2)(γ-1)/γ$. The estimates are uniform for uniformly bounded families of solutions whose source terms range over a bounded, uniformly equi-integrable subset of $L^{q_c}$. The main difficulty is the possible concentration of the critical gradient energy. We overcome it through a two-scale blow-up argument: the first scale produces a small Hamiltonian coefficient, while energy normalization at the second scale yields strong endpoint compactness, allowing a parabolic Liouville theorem to rule out concentration. The resulting uniform little-Hölder estimate, combined with critical Gagliardo--Nirenberg interpolation, permits absorption of the nonlinear term in the parabolic Calderón--Zygmund estimate. As an application, we construct bounded strong solutions for $f\in L^{q_c}$ and continuous initial data $u_0$, and prove subsequential strong interior convergence of smooth approximations in $W^{2,1}_{q_c}$.

</details>


### [85] [Logarithmic Bramson correction for Fisher--KPP equations on homogeneous trees](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33805)
*Edagardo Álvarez,Marina Murillo-Arcila,Rodrigo Ponce,Juan C. Pozo*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Studies the Fisher–KPP equation on a homogeneous (q+1)-regular tree, obtaining explicit linearized fundamental solution estimates, identifying the propagation threshold and critical parameters, and proving propagation of level sets with a logarithmic delay carrying the classical Bramson factor 3/2.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Classical Fisher–KPP propagation and logarithmic-delay (Bramson) phenomena are well understood on one-dimensional continuous and discrete domains, but whether they persist on non-Euclidean branching geometries such as homogeneous trees is unclear.

Method: Derive an explicit representation of the fundamental solution of the linearized problem on TT_{q+1}, with sharp two-sided pointwise estimates. For compactly supported radial initial data, analyze the nonlinear problem and track the outermost θ-level set in the regime β > α(√q − 1)^2.

Result: Sharp linearized estimates identify the propagation threshold β > α(√q−1)^2 and the associated critical speed c_* and decay rate λ_*. For every θ ∈ (0,1), the outermost θ-level set satisfies κ_θ(t) = c_* t − (3/(2λ_*)) log t + O(1) as t → ∞.

Conclusion: Although branching geometry alters the propagation threshold and critical parameters, the classical Bramson factor 3/2 persists, extending classical propagation and logarithmic-delay phenomena to homogeneous trees.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the Fisher--KPP equation \[ \partial_t u =
αΔ_{\TT_{q+1}}u+βu(1-u), \] on the homogeneous \((q+1)\)-regular tree \(\TT_{q+1}\), with \(q\geq2\), for nontrivial, compactly supported radial initial data. We first obtain an explicit representation of the fundamental solution of the corresponding linearized problem, together with sharp two-sided pointwise estimates. These estimates identify the propagation threshold \[ β>α(\sqrt q-1)^2 \] and determine the associated critical speed \(c_\ast\) and decay rate \(λ_\ast\). In this regime, we prove that, for every fixed \(θ\in(0,1)\), the outermost \(θ\)-level set satisfies \[ κ_θ(t) =
c_\ast t-\frac{3}{2λ_\ast}\log t+O(1), \qquad t\to\infty. \] Therefore, while the branching geometry affects the threshold for propagation and the values of the critical parameters, the classical Bramson factor \(3/2\) persists. Our results therefore extend classical propagation and logarithmic-delay phenomena, previously known for one-dimensional continuous and discrete Fisher--KPP models, to homogeneous trees.

</details>


### [86] [Stability of 3D Stratified Plane Couette Flow: A New Lift-up Mechanism and Potential Vorticity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33960)
*Yiting Yao*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies stability of the 3D Boussinesq system near plane Couette flow with perpendicular vertical stratification, revealing new non-parallel structural effects (nonlocal coupling and wave-shear coupled lift-up) and establishing inviscid damping, linear stability of tilted Couette flow, and nonlinear stability via a potential vorticity reformulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Since Taylor and Goldstein (1931), stratified shear flow studies have focused on parallel configurations, while non-parallel configurations (where shear and stratification directions are perpendicular) remain poorly understood. This geometry introduces structural effects absent in the parallel case, motivating a systematic stability analysis.

Method: Analyzes the 3D Boussinesq system linearized around plane Couette flow V*=(y,0,0) with vertical stratification η*=αz. Uses a reformulation based on linearized potential vorticity (PV) to recover a better energy structure for the nonzero-mode system, and studies zero modes via a non-parallel/wave-shear coupled lift-up mechanism. Applies the method to tilted Couette flow and analyzes nonlinear stability for Sobolev perturbations on T×R×T with weighted norms.

Result: The non-parallel geometry produces two structural effects: an additional nonlocal coupling in the nonzero-mode system (preventing valid symmetrization without reformulation) and a non-parallel/wave-shear coupled lift-up for zero modes. L^p transient growth from lift-up is suppressed by sufficiently strong stratification (p>2) or suitable initial data assumptions (p≥2). Inviscid damping of u_{1,≠} and u_{2,≠} is established, and the method extends to tilted Couette flow. Nonlinear stability of plane Couette flow is proved, where transient growth arises only through nonlinear interactions and nonlinear lift-up is suppressed by strong stratification.

Conclusion: The PV-based reformulation provides a powerful framework for analyzing non-parallel stratified shear flows, yielding inviscid damping and both linear and nonlinear stability results. Strong stratification suppresses lift-up-induced transient growth, and weighted Sobolev data choices control nonlinear transient growth.

Abstract: Since the pioneering work of Taylor and Goldstein in 1931 laid the foundation for the study of stratified shear flows, non-parallel configurations have received comparatively little attention. In this article, we study the stability of the three-dimensional Boussinesq system near the plane Couette flow $V^*=(y,0,0)$ under the vertically stratified background $η^*=αz$. The shear and stratification directions are perpendicular. This geometry produces two structural effects that are absent in the parallel configuration: an additional nonlocal coupling in the nonzero-mode system, which prevents the system from having a valid symmetrization structure without reformulation, and a non-parallel/wave-shear coupled lift-up mechanism for the zero modes. The $L^p$ transient growth due to the lift-up effect can be suppressed either by sufficiently strong stratification (when $p>2$), or under suitable assumptions on the initial data (when $p\geq 2$). To study the nonzero-mode system, we introduce a reformulation based on the linearized potential vorticity (PV), a fundamental quantity in geophysical fluid dynamics, to recover a better energy structure. We establish the inviscid damping of $u_{1,\neq}$ and $u_{2,\neq}$. We further apply this method to study the linear stability of the tilted Couette flow. Based on the new formulation through PV, we also investigate the nonlinear stability of the plane Couette flow for Sobolev perturbations on $\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}$. We consider initial data with finite weighted Sobolev norm, where the weight is chosen to suppress the lift-up effect at the linear level, so that transient growth arises only through nonlinear interactions and the nonlinear lift-up can be suppressed by strong stratification.

</details>


### [87] [PV-Wave Decomposition and Combined Wave Effects for Stratified Couette Flows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33961)
*Yiting Yao*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: A "PV-Wave" decomposition constructs adapted energy variables for stability analysis of Couette flows, applied to 3D Boussinesq MHD and Rotating Boussinesq systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Inspired by a class of three-component Fourier ODE systems where a conserved mode couples to a pair of oscillatory modes, the paper seeks adapted energy variables for stability analysis of Couette flows.

Method: The PV-Wave decomposition separates the conserved potential-vorticity component, symmetrizes the remaining wave subsystem, and adds a correction to control couplings from time-dependent Fourier coefficients.

Result: The framework is applied to linearized 3D Boussinesq MHD and Rotating Boussinesq Couette flows, quantifying inviscid damping and parameter-dependent amplification from Alfvén-gravity and inertial-gravity wave interactions.

Conclusion: The PV-Wave strategy provides an effective tool for constructing adapted energy variables and characterizing wave-driven dynamics in Couette flow stability analysis.

Abstract: Motivated by a class of three-component Fourier ODE systems consisting of a conserved mode coupled to a pair of oscillatory modes, we develop a "potential-vorticity-wave" (PV-Wave) decomposition strategy for constructing adapted energy variables in the stability analysis of Couette flows. The strategy separates the conserved potential-vorticity component, symmetrizes the remaining wave subsystem, and introduces an additional correction to control the couplings generated by time-dependent Fourier coefficients. We apply this framework to study the linearized dynamics of 3D Boussinesq MHD system and Rotating Boussinesq system for Couette flows and quantify the inviscid damping and parameter-dependent amplification arising from the interaction of Alfvén-gravity waves and inertial-gravity waves.

</details>


### [88] [Collapse of white dwarfs above the Chandrasekhar mass](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34075)
*Gong Chen,Zhiwu Lin*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper provides a dynamical characterization of the Chandrasekhar mass for cold white dwarfs via radial Euler-Poisson equations, showing it is the threshold mass for finite-time complete collapse.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Chandrasekhar mass is classically a static/equilibrium quantity, but its dynamical meaning as a collapse threshold was not rigorously characterized. The authors seek to establish it dynamically from the radial Euler-Poisson equations with the exact Chandrasekhar equation of state.

Method: Matched asymptotic expansions combined with local existence theory and energy estimates that extend solutions backward from near-collapse times to a common earlier time. The analysis uses rescaling to capture the interior (collapsing Goldreich-Weber profile) and the near-vacuum boundary behavior, and constructs solutions above the critical mass.

Result: The Chandrasekhar mass is shown to be the infimum of masses admitting a finite-energy classical solution with physical vacuum that collapses its entire support to a point in finite time. Below it, energy conservation prevents complete collapse. For masses just above it, solutions are constructed whose support shrinks to the origin, with density concentrating there carrying the full stellar mass; the interior approaches a collapsing Goldreich-Weber profile, and a distinct boundary limiting profile is determined. Adding electron rest energy makes the conserved energy equal that of the Goldreich-Weber reference solution.

Conclusion: The Chandrasekhar mass admits a dynamical characterization as the sharp threshold for finite-time complete gravitational collapse of cold white dwarfs, valid for the exact Chandrasekhar equation of state.

Abstract: We give a dynamical characterization of the Chandrasekhar mass for cold white dwarfs governed by the radial Euler--Poisson equations with the exact Chandrasekhar equation of state. This mass is the infimum of masses for which a finite-energy classical solution with a physical vacuum can contract its entire support to a point in finite time. Below it, conservation of energy prevents complete collapse. For every mass in a sufficiently small interval above it, we construct a solution whose support shrinks to the origin and whose density concentrates there with the full mass of the star. The interior approaches a collapsing Goldreich--Weber profile. Near the vacuum boundary, a different rescaling gives a limiting profile that connects the high- and low-density regimes of the pressure law. We determine this profile and show that, after adding the electron rest energy, the conserved energy equals that of the Goldreich--Weber reference solution. The proof combines matched asymptotic expansions with local existence and energy estimates that extend solutions backward from times approaching collapse to a common earlier time.

</details>


### [89] [Long-time continuum limits for twisted Schrödinger lattices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34081)
*Brian Choi*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves polynomial-in-time Sobolev bounds on the error between the twisted discrete NLS and its magnetic continuum limit, establishing a focusing-defocusing dichotomy in uniform control.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how well a non-integrable finite-difference lattice approximates an integrable continuum flow, and under what data conditions uniform approximation is possible.

Method: Exploits complete integrability of the continuum flow via its Birkhoff representation; combines Birkhoff coordinates with modified energies for discrete Sobolev growth, and uses Fourier filtering to obtain uniform spacetime estimates below the energy space.

Result: Polynomial-in-time Sobolev bounds hold for arbitrary defocusing data and focusing data of sufficiently small mass; obstructions arise for large focusing data due to modulational instability and aliasing. Spatial rates are generically optimal in the Baire-category sense.

Conclusion: Uniform polynomial control of the discrete-to-continuum approximation exhibits a focusing-defocusing dichotomy, with optimal generic rates and spacetime estimates achievable below the energy space.

Abstract: We prove polynomial-in-time Sobolev bounds on the approximation error between the twisted discrete nonlinear Schrödinger equation and its magnetic continuum limit.
