The analysis exploits complete integrability of the continuum flow through its Birkhoff representation to control approximation by a non-integrable Hamiltonian finite-difference lattice. We establish a focusing-defocusing dichotomy in uniform polynomial control: the bounds hold for arbitrary defocusing data and focusing data of sufficiently small mass, while modulational instability and aliasing yield an obstruction for large focusing data. The accompanying algebraic spatial rates are generically optimal in the Baire-category sense. The proof combines Birkhoff coordinates with modified energies for discrete Sobolev growth, while Fourier filtering recovers uniform spacetime estimates below the energy space despite degenerate lattice dispersion.

</details>


### [90] [Nonlinear Stability of Nonconstant Steady States in Chemotaxis--Consumption Systems with General Motility](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34202)
*Xinyu Cheng,Xuewen Lai,Xu Song*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves nonlinear stability of positive nonconstant steady states for a chemotaxis-consumption system with nutrient-dependent motility and fixed boundary nutrient concentration, showing exponential convergence under large nutrient diffusivity in 2D/3D bounded domains.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding stability of nonconstant steady states in chemotaxis-consumption systems with nutrient-dependent motility, where steady state structure and convergence under conserved mass are not fully characterized, especially on general domains and with quantified asymptotics.

Method: Analysis of a chemotaxis-consumption PDE system with motility satisfying two monotonicity conditions (power laws, saturating functions). Existence and uniqueness of positive steady state per prescribed mass for large nutrient diffusivity D; nonlinear stability analysis via compatible perturbations; asymptotic expansion at order D^{-1} using the torsion function.

Result: For each prescribed population mass, sufficiently large D yields a unique positive steady state. Small compatible perturbations generate unique global solutions converging exponentially to that steady state with conserved mass. Holds on general bounded 2D/3D domains (lower-regularity version on an interval). At order D^{-1}, the nutrient deficit equals the torsion function times mean population density and boundary nutrient value; population contrast depends on κ_b = bφ'(b)/φ(b).

Conclusion: The system exhibits robust nonlinear stability with exponential relaxation, with explicit leading-order asymptotics in D^{-1} for nutrient deficit and population contrast; numerics confirm relaxation from compatible data and convergence to distinct steady states as initial mass varies.

Abstract: We prove nonlinear stability of positive nonconstant steady states for a chemotaxis--consumption system with nutrient-dependent motility and a fixed boundary nutrient concentration $b$. The motility satisfies two monotonicity conditions met by power laws and saturating functions. For each prescribed population mass, sufficiently large nutrient diffusivity $D$ yields a unique positive steady state. Small compatible perturbations generate unique global solutions converging exponentially to the steady state with their conserved mass. The result holds on general bounded domains in two and three dimensions, with a lower-regularity version on an interval. At order $D^{-1}$, the nutrient deficit is the torsion function multiplied by the mean population density and boundary nutrient value; the population contrast also depends on $κ_b=bφ'(b)/φ(b)$. Computations illustrate relaxation from compatible data and convergence to different steady states when the initial mass changes.

</details>


### [91] [Unique continuation, nonexistence and bubbling for the critical $p$-Laplace equation in the plane](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34204)
*Carlo Mercuri*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: For 1<p<2 in planar domains, weak and strong unique continuation is proven for Δ_p u + f(u)=0 with |f(s)|≤C|s|^{p-1}, yielding nonexistence of nontrivial finite-energy solutions for the critical p-Laplace equation on a half-plane and a bubble-profile decomposition of Palais-Smale sequences.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Unique continuation and related nonexistence results were previously known mainly for p=2 (e.g., Esteban-Lions, Struwe). Extending these to the p-Laplacian for p≠2, especially in the plane, was a long-standing open problem, with the Struwe-type decomposition still open in dimension N≥3 for p≠2.

Method: The authors prove weak and strong unique continuation properties for solutions of Δ_p u + f(u)=0 in a planar domain, assuming f continuous with |f(s)|≤C|s|^{p-1}. These are then applied to the critical p-Laplace equation with zero Dirichlet boundary conditions on a half-plane and to the analysis of Palais-Smale sequences for the Brezis-Nirenberg problem.

Result: A solution vanishing on an open set vanishes identically, and one vanishing to infinite order at a point does too. Consequently, for the critical p-Laplace equation with zero Dirichlet boundary condition on a half-plane, there are no nontrivial finite-energy solutions. Unique continuation provides the missing ingredient for a planar generalization to the p-Laplacian of Struwe's bubble-profile decomposition of possibly sign-changing Palais-Smale sequences for the Brezis-Nirenberg problem. Loss of compactness is characterized via finite-energy solutions of Δ_p u + |u|^{p*-2}u = 0 in R^2.

Conclusion: Unique continuation for the planar p-Laplacian enables progress on long-standing p=2 results (Esteban-Lions, Struwe), establishing nonexistence of finite-energy solutions for the critical p-Laplace equation in a half-plane and a characterization of compactness loss for the Brezis-Nirenberg problem in the plane. The N≥3, p≠2 case remains open.

Abstract: For $1<p<2$ we prove weak and strong unique continuation properties for the solutions to $Δ_pu+f(u)=0$ in a planar domain, with $f$ continuous and such that $|f(s)|\leq C|s|^{p-1}$: a solution vanishing on an open set vanishes identically, and so does a solution vanishing to infinite order at a single point. We can therefore make progress towards a proof of some long-standing nonexistence results available only for $p=2$, such as a celebrated one of Esteban and Lions, establishing here that for the critical $p$-Laplace equation with zero Dirichlet boundary condition on a half-plane, there are no nontrivial finite energy solutions. In the plane, unique continuation thus provides the missing ingredient for a generalisation to the $p$-Laplacian operator of a classical result of Struwe on the bubble-profile decomposition of possibly sign-changing Palais-Smale sequences associated to the Brezis-Nirenberg problem, which remains open in dimension $N\geq3$ for $p\neq2$. We obtain a characterisation of their loss of compactness in terms of the finite energy solutions of $Δ_pu+|u|^{p^*-2}u=0$ in $\mathbb R^2$.

</details>


### [92] [Boundary Regularity for Fully Nonlinear Singular Elliptic Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34238)
*Jiqi Dong,Xuemei Li*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes boundary regularity for the singular fully nonlinear elliptic equation |Du|^γ F(D²u)=f with -1<γ<0, proving pointwise boundary C^{1,α} estimates under weaker domain assumptions and extending W^{2,δ} estimates to C^{1,α} domains.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior work by Birindelli-Demengel required C² domain smoothness for boundary C^{1,α} estimates, and Li-Li's W^{2,δ} estimates were restricted to balls. The authors seek to weaken domain regularity assumptions and extend these estimates to more general domains.

Method: First, pointwise boundary C^{1,α} estimates are proved via compactness and perturbation arguments without flattening the boundary, requiring only pointwise C^{1,α} Dirichlet data. Second, the W^{2,δ} estimates are extended from balls to C^{1,α} domains by combining pointwise boundary C^{1,α} estimates, interior W^{2,δ₀} estimates, and a Whitney decomposition.

Result: Pointwise boundary C^{1,α} estimates hold with pointwise C^{1,α} Dirichlet data, weakening the C² smoothness requirement. The W^{2,δ} estimates are extended to C^{1,α} domains with C^{1,α} Dirichlet data for 0<δ≤δ₀<1.

Conclusion: Boundary regularity for the singular elliptic equation is established under weaker regularity assumptions on both the domain and Dirichlet data, generalizing previous results of Birindelli-Demengel and Li-Li.

Abstract: We study boundary regularity for the fully nonlinear singular elliptic equation of the form $|Du|^γF(D^2u)=f$, where $-1<γ<0$. First, we prove pointwise boundary $C^{1,α}$ estimates with pointwise $C^{1,α}$ Dirichlet data, which weakens the $C^2$ domain smoothness requirement from Birindelli and Demengel~\cite{BD10}. The proof uses compactness and perturbation, without flattening the boundary. Second, we extend the $W^{2,δ}$ estimates of Li and Li~\cite{LiLi17} from balls to $C^{1,α}$ domains with $C^{1,α}$ Dirichlet data{, for $0<δ\leδ_0<1$. Here $δ_0$ is an exponent in the interior $W^{2,δ_0}$ estimate}. The proof combines pointwise boundary $C^{1,α}$ estimates, interior $W^{2,δ_0}$ estimates, and a Whitney decomposition.

</details>


### [93] [A Critical Value for the Viscosity Coefficient: Triggering Oscillatory Traveling Waves in the Pseudo-parabolic Fisher-KPP Equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34368)
*Yang Cao,Haomeng Chen,Chunhua Jin,Jingxue Yin*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper shows that the pseudo-parabolic term in the Fisher-KPP equation induces a sharp transition in traveling wave structure controlled by the ratio τ/D.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The pseudo-parabolic term u_xxt is a canonical higher-order viscosity that provides effective regularization, but how it alters the dynamics of prototype equations remains not fully understood.

Method: Study the pseudo-parabolic Fisher-KPP equation u_t - τu_xxt = D u_xx + u(1-u), characterizing the role of the parameter ratio τ/D, supported by numerical simulations.

Result: The ratio τ/D acts as a critical switch: monotonic classical traveling waves are preserved for τ/D ≤ 1, while oscillatory traveling waves emerge for τ/D > 1, with the threshold at τ/D = 1.

Conclusion: The pseudo-parabolic term plays a dual role—structure-preserving viscosity when small, and dominant capillary hysteresis when large, explaining saturation overshoot observed in two-phase flow that contradicts classical diffusion models.

Abstract: The pseudo-parabolic term $u_{xxt}$ serves as a canonical example of higher-order viscosity that provides effective regularization while capturing the refined physical mechanism. How it alters the dynamics of prototype equations remains a fundamental issue, which is not yet fully understood. The goal of this paper is to show that this term induces a sharp transition in traveling wave structure, via studying the pseudo-parabolic Fisher-KPP equation $$ u_t - τu_{xxt} = D u_{xx} + u(1-u). $$ We completely characterize how the parameter ratio $τ/D$ acts as a critical switch: it preserves the monotonic structure of classical traveling waves when $τ/D \leq 1$, yet it triggers a qualitative shift to oscillatory traveling waves when $τ/D > 1$. Numerical simulations support these findings. The threshold $τ/D=1$ thus captures the dual role of the pseudo-parabolic term: it acts as a structure-preserving viscosity when small, but when large it reflects dominant capillary hysteresis, generating the oscillations that explain the saturation overshoot, a phenomenon that contradicts classical diffusion models yet is widely observed in two-phase flow.

</details>


### [94] [The fractional Fisher-KPP equation with free boundaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34504)
*Huyuan Chen,Yihong Du,Wenjie Ni*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes global existence and uniqueness for a fractional diffusion free boundary problem modeling species spreading, then proves a spreading-vanishing dichotomy for Fisher-KPP growth.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Extends prior free boundary models: the nonlocal integrable-kernel model (CDLL2019) and the classical Laplacian model (DuLin2010). The well-posedness question for the fractional Laplacian $-\\Delta)^s$ case has remained open due to kernel singularity/non-integrability and lack of sufficiently general regularity results.

Method: Uses an approximation approach: an associated linear fractional parabolic initial-boundary value problem with curved boundaries is broken into a sequence of approximating problems where curved-boundary functions are replaced by approximating step functions.

Result: Global existence and uniqueness of the solution are established (main contribution). For Fisher-KPP type nonlinear growth, the long-time dynamics exhibit a spreading-vanishing dichotomy, analogous to DuLin2010 and CDLL2019.

Conclusion: Well-posedness of the fractional-diffusion free boundary problem is resolved, and the spreading-vanishing dichotomy is confirmed; finer description of the spreading profile is deferred to a separate work.

Abstract: In this paper, we consider a free boundary problem with fractional diffusion $(-Δ)^s$ $(0<s<1)$, as a model for species spreading, which can be viewed as a natural extension of the free boundary model in \cite{CDLL2019}, where the nonlocal diffusion term is defined via a continuous integrable kernel function $J(x)$. This fractional diffusion model can also be viewed as a nonlocal version of the free boundary model in \cite{DuLin2010}, whose local diffusion term is given by the classical Laplacian.
  We first establish the global existence and uniqueness of the solution, which is the main contribution of this paper. This well-posedness question has been open for some time now due to the difficulties caused by the singularity and non-integrability of the associated kernel function of $(-Δ)^s$, and the lack of general enough regularity results for operators extending the standard fractional Laplacian. A crucial step that enables us to answer this question relies on an approximation approach, which breaks an associated linear fractional parabolic initial boundary value problem with curved boundaries into a sequence of approximating problems, where the functions representing the curved boundaries are replaced by approximating step functions.
  We then show that, for Fisher-KPP type nonlinear growth terms, the long-time dynamics of the model exhibits a spreading-vanishing dichotomy, similar to the earlier models of \cite{DuLin2010, CDLL2019}. More precise descriptions of the spreading profile of the solution will be considered in a separate work.

</details>


### [95] [Entropy instability and rigidity for the exponential semilinear heat equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34541)
*Yuxia Guo,Jionghao Lv*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Every non-constant bounded self-similar solution of the exponential semilinear heat equation is entropy unstable; the trivial profile w≡0 is quantitatively isolated among bounded solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivated by recent developments in mean curvature flow, the paper aims to understand bounded self-similar solutions of the exponential semilinear heat equation through variational tools (the F-functional and entropy).

Method: Uses the F-functional and entropy as variational/rigidity tools, analyzing the self-similar equation for the exponential semilinear heat equation.

Result: Non-constant bounded self-similar solutions are shown to be entropy unstable, and the trivial profile w≡0 is quantitatively isolated among bounded solutions.

Conclusion: Provides a variational instability theorem for nontrivial profiles and a rigidity theorem for the trivial profile.

Abstract: In this paper, we study bounded self-similar solutions for the exponential semilinear heat equation via the $F$-functional and entropy. Motivated by recent developments in mean curvature flow, we prove that every non-constant bounded solution of the self-similar equation is entropy unstable. We also show that the trivial profile $w\equiv0$ is quantitatively isolated among bounded solutions. These results provide both a variational instability theorem for nontrivial profiles and a rigidity theorem for the trivial profile.

</details>


### [96] [Scale-invariant bounds on thin sets for measures satisfying a first-order PDE and the anisotropic Michael-Simon inequality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34573)
*Guido De Philippis,Luca Gennaioli,Alessandro Pigati,Filip Rindler*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves mass estimates for PDE-constrained measures on σ-finite H^m-measurable sets under a rank condition, and derives a Michael-Simon inequality for varifolds with bounded anisotropic first variation satisfying the atomic condition (AC1).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Extending mass estimate and Michael-Simon inequality theory to general PDE-constrained measures and anisotropic varifolds requires handling rank conditions and natural integrand assumptions not covered by earlier Euclidean/isotropic results.

Method: Establish mass estimates for PDE-constrained measures supported on σ-finite H^m-measure sets, assuming an appropriate rank condition; apply these to varifolds via the atomic condition (AC1) for the anisotropic integrand.

Result: Mass estimates are proven under the rank condition, and a Michael-Simon inequality is obtained for varifolds with bounded anisotropic first variation.

Conclusion: The results generalize mass-estimate and Michael-Simon type inequalities to the anisotropic, PDE-constrained setting.

Abstract: We prove mass estimates for PDE-constrained measures on sets of $σ$-finite ${\mathcal H}^m$-measure, assuming an appropriate rank condition. As an application, we derive a Michael--Simon inequality for varifolds with bounded anisotropic first variation with respect to an integrand that satisfies the natural atomic condition (AC1).

</details>


### [97] [Sharp linear stability and the absence of enhanced dissipation for Kolmogorov flow in the 2D Navier--Stokes equations with horizontal dissipation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34610)
*Wanrong Yang,Jiahong Wu,Xiaoping Zhai*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper analyzes linearized dynamics of Kolmogorov flow under horizontal-only dissipation, showing no enhanced dissipation and identifying stability conditions across Fourier modes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Unlike the fully dissipative case, the anisotropic system with only horizontal dissipation admits Kolmogorov flow as an exact unforced steady state, motivating a study of its linearized stability and decay properties.

Method: The linearized semigroup is factored exactly into e^{-νk^2t} times the inviscid Euler group on each horizontal Fourier mode k, since dissipation reduces to scalar -νk^2 and commutes with advection. Two-sided bounds and spectral analysis are used across mode regimes.

Result: For |k|>1, decay rate is exactly νk^2 uniformly in shear amplitude a (no enhanced dissipation). At |k|=1, inviscid group grows like √(2at), giving transient amplification of size (a/eν)^{1/2} before decay at rate ν. Horizontally independent modes form an infinite-dimensional undamped kernel. For 0<|k|<1, mode is linearly unstable iff aΛ(k)>νk^2; when L>2π, flow is linearly unstable for all sufficiently large a.

Conclusion: Horizontal dissipation fails to produce enhanced dissipation because shear transfers enstrophy to vertical frequencies that horizontal dissipation cannot detect, revealing a fundamental mismatch; stability depends on mode and shear amplitude.

Abstract: We study the linearized dynamics of the Kolmogorov flow $U^{(0)}=(a\sin x_2,0)$ for the two-dimensional incompressible Navier--Stokes equations with horizontal dissipation $ν\partial_1^2$. Unlike the fully dissipative case, this anisotropic system admits $U^{(0)}$ as an exact unforced steady state. On each horizontal Fourier mode $k$, the dissipation reduces to the scalar $-νk^2$ and commutes with the advection, so that the linearized semigroup factors exactly into $e^{-νk^2t}$ times the inviscid Euler group. For $|k|>1$, we prove two-sided bounds showing that the decay rate is exactly $νk^2$, uniformly in the shear amplitude $a$; hence no enhanced dissipation occurs. This reflects a fundamental mismatch: the shear transfers enstrophy to high vertical frequencies, which horizontal dissipation does not detect. At the critical modes $|k|=1$, the inviscid group grows like $\sqrt{2at}$, producing a transient amplification of size $(a/eν)^{1/2}$ before decay at the rate $ν$. The horizontally independent modes form an infinite-dimensional undamped kernel. For $0<|k|<1$, the viscous spectrum is an exact translate of the inviscid one, and the mode is linearly unstable if and only if $aΛ(k)>νk^2$. In particular, when $L>2π$, the flow is linearly unstable for all sufficiently large $a$.

</details>


### [98] [A Critical one-component regularity criteria for the three-dimensional Navier--Stokes equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34637)
*Guo Maotuo,Xiong Shiyang,Zhao Chuankai*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves two critical one-component regularity criteria for the 3D incompressible Navier–Stokes equations, extending prior work by relaxing dyadic summability in the endpoint case and removing an extra initial-vorticity integrability assumption in the nonendpoint range.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing critical one-component regularity criteria (Han–Lei–Li–Zhao) impose restrictive conditions: a dyadic square-summability (H^{3/2}=B^{3/2}_{2,2}) and an extra L^{q_0} integrability assumption on the initial vorticity. The goal is to relax these conditions and broaden the class of admissible data.

Method: At the endpoint p=2, a new vertical-vorticity estimate is combined with coupled anisotropic energy estimates and an energy-dependent frequency decomposition, relaxing B^{3/2}_{2,2} to B^{3/2}_{2,r} (2<r≤∞), with r=∞ closed by an Osgood-type argument. In the nonendpoint range 2<p<∞, a positive-time heat-flow decomposition splits the solution into a smooth linear part and a nonlinear remainder with finite energy.

Result: For divergence-free u_0∈H^1(R^3), a strong solution extends beyond T if u^3∈L^2(0,T;B^{3/2}_{2,r}(R^3)), 2<r≤∞. For every 2<p<∞, if u_0∈H^{1/2}(R^3) and u·e∈L^p(0,T;H^{1/2+2/p}(R^3)) for some fixed unit vector e, the Fujita–Kato solution extends beyond T without additional low-integrability assumptions on the initial vorticity.

Conclusion: The paper extends critical one-component regularity criteria in two directions: relaxing the dyadic square-summability to Besov-type B^{3/2}_{2,r} and removing the extra L^{q_0} initial-vorticity assumption while retaining the natural critical space H^{1/2}(R^3).

Abstract: We establish two critical one-component regularity criteria for the three-dimensional incompressible Navier--Stokes equations. First, for divergence-free initial data $u_0\in H^1(\mathbb R^3)$, we prove that a strong solution can be continued beyond a finite time $T$ provided that \[
  u^3\in L^2\bigl(0,T;\dot B^{3/2}_{2,r}(\mathbb R^3)\bigr)
  \qquad\text{for some }2<r\leq\infty. \] Second, for every $2<p<\infty$, if $u_0\in\dot H^{1/2}(\mathbb R^3)$ and, for some fixed unit vector $\boldsymbol e\in\mathbb S^2$, \[
  u\cdot\boldsymbol e
  \in
  L^p\bigl(0,T;\dot H^{1/2+2/p}(\mathbb R^3)\bigr), \] then the corresponding Fujita--Kato solution also extends beyond $T$, without any additional low-integrability assumption on the initial vorticity.
  These results extend the critical one-component regularity criteria of Han, Lei, Li, and Zhao in two different directions. At the time endpoint $p=2$, we combine a new vertical-vorticity estimate with coupled anisotropic energy estimates and an energy-dependent frequency decomposition to relax the dyadic square-summability condition \[
  \dot H^{3/2}=\dot B^{3/2}_{2,2} \] to \[
  \dot B^{3/2}_{2,r},
  \qquad 2<r\leq\infty, \] with the endpoint case $r=\infty$ closed by an Osgood-type argument. In the nonendpoint range $2<p<\infty$, we use a positive-time heat-flow decomposition to separate the solution into a smooth linear part and a nonlinear remainder. The remainder has finite energy and acquires the required $L^q$-integrability of the vorticity at positive times, which allows the anisotropic energy estimates to be applied while the additional transport, stretching, and pressure terms generated by the heat flow remain integrable. This removes the extra $L^{q_0}$-integrability assumption on the initial vorticity while retaining the natural critical initial-data space $\dot H^{1/2}(\mathbb R^3)$.

</details>


### [99] [The limit of the optimal Li--Yau constant for the fractional heat equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34700)
*Huaiqian Li*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This note proves the affirmative answer to Weber–Zacher's question: the optimal Li–Yau constant $C_{\rm LY}(\beta,d)$ for the fractional heat equation converges to the classical Li–Yau constant $d/2$ as $\beta\uparrow2$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Weber and Zacher introduced the optimal constant $C_{\rm LY}(\beta,d)$ in a Li–Yau type gradient estimate for the fractional heat equation and asked whether it converges to the classical Li–Yau constant $d/2$ as $\beta\uparrow2$. This paper resolves that question.

Method: Two main ingredients: (1) the Bochner subordination formula with a concavity argument showing the supremum in Weber–Zacher's formula for $C_{\rm LY}(\beta,d)$ is attained at the origin; (2) a lower bound for the fractional heat kernel profile in the large-distance regime, using the Lévy–Itô decomposition to derive the factor $2-\beta$.

Result: For every fixed $d\geq1$, there exists $K_d>0$ such that $\frac{d}{\beta}\leq C_{\rm LY}(\beta,d)\leq\frac{d}{\beta}+K_d(2-\beta)\log\frac{e}{2-\beta}$ for $1\leq\beta<2$. Consequently, $\lim_{\beta\uparrow2}C_{\rm LY}(\beta,d)=d/2$.

Conclusion: The classical Li–Yau constant $d/2$ is recovered as the limit of the fractional optimal constants $C_{\rm LY}(\beta,d)$ as $\beta\uparrow2$, confirming Weber–Zacher's conjecture.

Abstract: For the fractional heat equation $\partial_t u + (-Δ)^{β/2}u=0$ on $\mathbb{R}^d$ with $β\in(0,2)$, Weber and Zacher introduced the optimal constant $C_{\rm LY}(β,d)$ in the Li--Yau type estimate $$(-Δ)^{β/2}\log u(t,\cdot)\leq \frac {C_{\rm LY}(β,d)}{t},\quad t>0.$$ They asked whether $C_{\rm LY}(β,d)$ converges to the classical Li--Yau constant $d/2$ as $β\uparrow2$. In this note we prove that the answer is affirmative. More precisely, for every fixed $d\geq1$ there exists a constant $K_d>0$ such that $$\frac{d}β\leq C_{\rm LY}(β,d)\leq\frac{d}β+K_d(2-β)\log\frac{e}{2-β},\quad 1\leqβ<2.$$ Consequently, $\lim_{β\uparrow2}C_{\rm LY}(β,d)=d/2$. The proof has two main ingredients. First, the Bochner subordination formula, together with a concavity argument, shows that the supremum in Weber--Zacher's formula for $C_{\rm LY}(β,d)$ is attained at the origin. Second, the factor $2-β$ is derived from a lower bound for the fractional heat kernel profile in the large-distance regime, where the Lévy--Itô decomposition plays an important role.

</details>


### [100] [A rigidity result for a global vectorial free boundary problem with a linear datum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34747)
*Giovanni Siclari,Bozhidar Velichkov*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper fully classifies minimizers of a measure-constrained global vectorial free boundary problem with linear datum, proving a unique minimizer whose coincidence set is an ellipsoid.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivated by connections between this problem and the blow-ups of the vectorial Bernoulli free boundary problem and the obstacle problem.

Method: Classification of minimizers of a measure constraint global vectorial free boundary problem with a linear datum.

Result: There exists a unique minimizer, and its coincidence set is an ellipsoid.

Conclusion: The minimizers are completely classified, with the unique minimizer characterized by an ellipsoidal coincidence set.

Abstract: In this paper we completely classify the minimizers of a measure constraint global vectorial free boundary problem with a linear datum. More precisely, we show that there exists a unique minimizer whose coincidence set is an ellipsoid. We are motivated by the connection of the problem with the blow-ups of the vectorial Bernoulli free boundary problem and the obstacle problem.

</details>


### [101] [A short note about the spaces $BV^s(\R^n)$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34760)
*Guillermo García-Sáez*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This short communication answers negatively a question raised by Brue et al. regarding the complex interpolation nature of the spaces BV^s(R^n) introduced by Comi and Stefani.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to resolve an open question posed in Brue2022 (Section 1.4) about whether the spaces BV^s(R^n) behave well under complex interpolation, a property relevant to their role as intermediate spaces.

Method: A short mathematical communication providing a counterexample / negative argument addressing the complex interpolation property of the Comi–Stefani spaces BV^s(R^n).

Result: The answer to the question is negative: the spaces BV^s(R^n) do not possess the claimed complex interpolation nature.

Conclusion: The complex interpolation question for BV^s(R^n) is settled negatively, refining understanding of these fractional bounded-variation spaces.

Abstract: In this short communication we answer negatively to a question posed by the authors in their work \cite[Section 1.4]{Brue2022} about the complex interpolation nature of the spaces $BV^{s}(\R^n)$, introduced by G.E. Comi and G. Stefani in \cite{ComiStefani2019}.

</details>


### [102] [Non-degeneracy and local uniqueness of bubbling solutions for a singular Liouville equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34815)
*Yuxia Guo,Jionghao Lv*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: For a singular Liouville equation on the unit disk, the authors prove non-degeneracy of bubbling solutions via local Pohozaev identities, then use it to establish local uniqueness of bubbling solutions with nearby concentration parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Bubbling solutions for singular Liouville equations are known to exist, but their non-degeneracy and uniqueness properties are not well understood. Local uniqueness requires understanding solution structure near concentration points.

Method: Local Pohozaev identities are applied to the bubbling solutions constructed in D-W-Z (2026) to prove a non-degeneracy result.

Result: The authors prove non-degeneracy of bubbling solutions and establish local uniqueness for bubbling solutions whose concentration parameters are sufficiently close.

Conclusion: Non-degeneracy via Pohozaev identities yields local uniqueness of bubbling solutions for the singular Liouville equation.

Abstract: We consider the following singular Liouville equation $-Δv=λV(x)|x|^2e^v \quad \text{in } B_1, \quad v=0 \quad \text{on } \partial B_1, $ where $B_1\subset\mathbb R^2$ is the unit disk, $λ>0$ is a small parameter, and $V$ is a positive smooth function. We first prove a non-degeneracy result for the bubbling solutions constructed in \cite{D-W-Z2026} by the local Pohozaev identities. Then we use this non-degeneracy result to establish the local uniqueness of bubbling solutions whose concentration parameters are sufficiently close.

</details>


### [103] [Asymptotically Hölder gradient regularity for degenerate or singular parabolic normalized $ p$-Laplace equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34855)
*Jiangwen Wang,Yini Zhang,Feida Jiang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves local spatial gradient Hölder regularity close to 1 for bounded viscosity solutions of a degenerate/singular parabolic equation driven by the normalized p-Laplacian, in the joint regime where p approaches 2 and γ approaches 0.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Extends the γ=0 result of Andrade and Santos (2022) to the joint regime (p,γ)→(2,0), answering a question raised in their Remark 1.1.

Method: Studies bounded viscosity solutions of u_t − |Du|^γ Δ_p^N u = f in Q_1, with normalized p-Laplacian Δ_p^N, −1<γ<∞, 1<p<∞, f continuous and bounded; uses perturbation arguments near the heat equation to establish C^{1+α,(1+α)/2} regularity.

Result: For any α∈(0,1), there exists ε=ε(n,α)>0 such that |p−2|+|γ|≤ε implies u∈C_loc^{1+α,(1+α)/2}(Q_1); the spatial Hölder exponent can be taken arbitrarily close to 1 as (p,γ)→(2,0).

Conclusion: The degenerate/singular parabolic equation admits arbitrarily high spatial gradient Hölder regularity near (p,γ)=(2,0), generalizing prior results and confirming the conjectured behavior.

Abstract: We study bounded viscosity solutions of the degenerate or singular parabolic equation \[
  u_t-|Du|^γΔ_p^{\mathrm{N}}u=f
  \qquad \text{in } Q_1, \] where \[
  Δ_p^{\mathrm{N}}u
  :=
  Δu+(p-2)
  \left\langle
  D^2u\frac{Du}{|Du|},
  \frac{Du}{|Du|}
  \right\rangle,
  \qquad
  -1<γ<\infty,\quad 1<p<\infty, \] and $f\in C^0(Q_1)\cap L^\infty(Q_1)$. Given any prescribed $α\in(0,1)$, we prove that there exists $\varepsilon=\varepsilon(n,α)>0$ such that \[
  |p-2|+|γ|\leq\varepsilon
  \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
  u\in C_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1+α,\frac{1+α}{2}}(Q_1). \] In particular, the spatial gradient Hölder exponent can be chosen arbitrarily close to $ 1 $ as $(p,γ)\to(2,0)$. This extends the result of Andrade and Santos (Calc. Var. Partial Differential Equations \textbf{61}, Paper No. 196, 2022) for $γ=0$ to the joint regime $(p,γ)\to(2,0)$ and answers affirmatively the question raised in Remark~1.1 therein.

</details>


### [104] [Well-posedness for time-fractional evolution equations by the Yosida approximation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35001)
*S. E. Chorfi,F. Gölgeleyen,M. Yamamoto*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves a fractional Hille-Yosida theorem characterizing unique existence of weak and strong solutions for a time-fractional evolution equation with Caputo derivative and a contraction semigroup generator.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Time-fractional evolution equations with Caputo-type derivatives generalize classical evolution equations, but a full characterization of well-posedness (analogous to the Hille-Yosida theorem) for such equations in Hilbert spaces was missing. The authors aim to fill this gap.

Method: The equation is $\partial_t^\alpha(u(t)-a)=Au(t)$ with $0<\alpha<1$, $A$ generating a contraction $C_0$ semigroup. The proofs construct an approximating sequence of solutions via the Yosida approximation.

Result: A fractional Hille-Yosida theorem characterizing unique existence of a weak solution for every $a\in X$; unique existence of a strong solution for $a\in\mathcal{D}(A)$; and continuity of weak solutions.

Conclusion: The framework applies to time-fractional transport equations and Boltzmann equations, providing a general well-posedness theory.

Abstract: We consider an initial value problem for a time-fractional evolution equation in a Hilbert space $X$: $$ \partial_t^α (u(t)-a) = Au(t) \qquad \mbox{for $0<t<T$}, $$ where $\partial_t^α$ denotes a fractional differential operator of Caputo type with order $0<α<1,$ $u\colon (0,T) \to X,$ $a$ describes an initial value, and $A$, with domain $\mathcal{D}(A),$ is the generator of a contraction C$_0$ semigroup in $X$. We prove a fractional Hille-Yosida theorem characterizing the unique existence of a weak solution for every $a\in X$. We also discuss the unique existence of a strong solution for $a \in \mathcal{D}(A),$ as well as the continuity of weak solutions. Our results are applicable, for example, to time-fractional transport equations and Boltzmann equations. The proofs are mainly based on constructing an approximating sequence of solutions using the Yosida approximation.

</details>


### [105] [Uniqueness of positive radial ground states for the massive pseudo-relativistic nonlinear Schrödinger equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35030)
*Zongyan Lv*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves uniqueness of the positive radial ground state for the semilinear pseudo-relativistic equation with mass m>0, via a new oscillation theorem for the linearized operator.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior uniqueness results (Frank–Lenzmann–Silvestre) cover the massless case m=0. For m>0 scale invariance fails, breaking a key orthogonality relation, so uniqueness for the massive pseudo-relativistic operator was open.

Method: A mass-covariant oscillation theorem for the linearized operator, proven through a purely variational reduction: the Caffarelli–Silvestre–Duffin extension for sqrt(-Δ+m^2) plus a ground-state substitution reduces the eigenvalue problem to a massless, potential-free weighted Steklov problem. Sign-change (once) of the second eigenfunction follows from strict superadditivity of weighted Dirichlet energy under sign decomposition. Nondegeneracy comes from two orthogonality relations, with the missing one repaired by a mass-covariance identity; global uniqueness follows by continuation in the mass parameter anchored at m=0.

Result: The positive radial variational ground state is unique for m>0, ω>0, d≥1 and 2<p<2*.

Conclusion: Uniqueness extends the massless Frank–Lenzmann–Silvestre theory to the massive setting, with the mass-covariance identity compensating for the loss of scale invariance.

Abstract: We study the semilinear pseudo-relativistic equation \[ \sqrt{-Δ+m^{2}}\,Q+ωQ=Q^{p-1}\qquad\text{in }\R^{d}, \] with $m>0$, $ω>0$, $d\ge1$ and $2<p<2^{*}:=\frac{2d}{d-1}$ (with $2^{*}=\infty$ when $d=1$). We prove that the positive radial variational ground state is unique. The proof rests on a mass-covariant oscillation theorem for the linearized operator, established through a purely variational reduction: the Caffarelli--Silvestre--Duffin extension for $\sqrt{-Δ+m^{2}}$ combined with a ground-state substitution reduces the linearized eigenvalue problem to a massless, potential-free weighted Steklov problem, whose second eigenfunction changes sign exactly once by strict superadditivity of the weighted Dirichlet energy under sign decomposition. The nondegeneracy of the linearization is then obtained from two orthogonality relations; the failure of scale invariance for $m>0$, which destroys the second relation used in the massless Frank--Lenzmann--Silvestre theory, is repaired by a mass-covariance identity. Global uniqueness follows by a continuation argument in the mass parameter, anchored at $m=0$ by the theorem of Frank--Lenzmann--Silvestre.

</details>


### [106] [On the singular layer for the 1-D steady Euler system with heat conductivity in a finite nozzle as the heat conduction coefficient vanishes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35033)
*Su Jiang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the vanishing heat-conductivity limit of the 1D steady compressible Euler system with heat conduction in a finite nozzle, showing existence of solutions, boundary layer formation, and classification into supersonic, subsonic, and transonic regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding how solutions of the steady compressible Euler system with heat conduction behave as the heat conductivity coefficient tends to zero is important for connecting viscous/conductive models to the ideal (inviscid, non-conducting) Euler limit and for characterizing boundary layer phenomena in nozzle flows.

Method: The authors establish existence of heat-conduction solutions for the 1D steady compressible Euler system in a finite nozzle, analyze the singular limit as conductivity vanishes, and perform a formal asymptotic expansion to derive the boundary layer equations. Solutions are classified according to the entrance state and exit pressure.

Result: Heat-conduction solutions exist, and boundary layers emerge at one or two nozzle endpoints in the zero-conductivity limit. The asymptotic behavior depends on the entrance state and exit pressure, yielding three regimes: purely supersonic, purely subsonic, and transonic solutions. Formal asymptotic expansions yield the corresponding boundary layer equations.

Conclusion: The vanishing heat-conductivity limit of the 1D steady compressible Euler system in a nozzle produces boundary layers and a classification of solutions into supersonic, subsonic, and transonic types, with boundary layer equations derived via formal asymptotics.

Abstract: This paper investigates the singular limit problem of the one-dimensional steady compressible Euler system with heat conduction in a finite nozzle. The existence of the heat conduction solutions were established and boundary layers at one or two nozzle endpoints occur as the coefficient of heat conductivity goes to zero. The asymptotic behavior of the solutions vary depending on the state at the entrance and the value of the pressure at the exit, which classify the solutions into purely supersonic solution, purely subsonic solution and transonic solution. Moreover, we perform a formal asymptotic expansion of solutions and derive the boundary layer equations.

</details>


### [107] [Multi-material flow with a new viscoelastic model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35172)
*S{é}bastien Boyaval*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper introduces a new quasilinear system of balance laws for front-capturing simulation of viscoelastic athermal multi-material flows with sharp interfaces, extending it with a thermodynamic energy balance and ensuring well-posedness via a symmetric-hyperbolic formulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Simulating viscoelastic athermal multi-material flows with sharp interfaces (e.g., mixtures of immiscible fluids and solids) is challenging, and existing models lack a suitable balance-law system that supports front-capturing while remaining thermodynamically consistent and well-posed.

Method: A new quasilinear system of balance laws (conservative first-order PDEs with source terms) is proposed, naturally extended with an energy balance relevant to stability and thermodynamics. A symmetric-hyperbolic formulation is adopted to guarantee an unequivocal definition of solutions to Cauchy problems.

Result: The system enables a front-capturing numerical approach for sharp-interface multi-material viscoelastic flows, and the symmetric-hyperbolic formulation guarantees an unequivocal definition of solutions to Cauchy problems in some cases.

Conclusion: The proposed balance-law system offers a viable, thermodynamically grounded framework for front-capturing simulation of viscoelastic athermal multi-material flows, with well-posedness ensured in certain cases.

Abstract: We study a new quasilinear system of balance laws (i.e. conservative first-order PDEs with source terms) for the numerical simulation by a front-capturing approach of viscoelastic athermal multi-material flows with sharp interfaces (like a mixture of immiscible fluids and solids). It naturally extends with an energy balance that is meaningful to stability and thermodynamics. In particular, the unequivocal definition of solutions to Cauchy problems can be guaranteed in some cases, thanks to a symmetric-hyperbolic formulation.

</details>


### [108] [Uniqueness of one-phase cones with isolated singularity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35175)
*Matteo Carducci,Bozhidar Velichkov*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves uniqueness of blow-ups at singular points of the one-phase free boundary problem whenever one blow-up has an isolated singularity, covering Lipschitz stationary solutions without integrability assumptions on the cone.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Uniqueness of tangent cones at isolated singularities was known in the minimal surface setting, but the one-phase free boundary problem lacked a complete picture, especially without integrability assumptions on the cone.

Method: Inspired by Simon's approach, the authors establish an infinite-dimensional Łojasiewicz inequality for the spherical Weiss energy, which implies an epiperimetric inequality for non-minimizing solutions and yields the uniqueness result.

Result: Uniqueness of the blow-up is proved at every singular point given that one blow-up has an isolated singularity, for Lipschitz stationary solutions.

Conclusion: The result completes the picture for uniqueness of tangent cones at isolated singularities in the one-phase problem and extends epiperimetric techniques to non-minimizing solutions.

Abstract: We prove uniqueness of the blow-up at every singular point of the one-phase free boundary problem for which one blow-up has an isolated singularity. The result applies to Lipschitz stationary solutions and does not require any integrability assumption on the cone. In this sense, our result completes the picture for uniqueness of tangent cones at isolated singularities in the one-phase problem.
  Inspired by Simon's work in the minimal surface setting, we prove an infinite dimensional Łojasiewicz inequality for the spherical Weiss' energy. This yields an epiperimetric inequality for non-minimizing solutions, which leads to the uniqueness result.

</details>


### [109] [A minimizing cone from Cartan polynomials in the Alt-Caffarelli problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35179)
*Matteo Carducci,Bozhidar Velichkov*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Studies 1-homogeneous solutions of the one-phase (Alt-Caffarelli) problem from Cartan isoparametric cubic polynomials (dimensions 5,8,14,26). Shows they are unstable in 5,8,14 and minimizing in 26, giving the first singular minimizing cone that is neither axially symmetric nor Lawson-type.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Singular minimizing cones in the Alt-Caffarelli problem were previously known only in axially symmetric or Lawson-type forms, leaving open whether other types of minimizing cones exist.

Method: Analyzes a class of 1-homogeneous solutions of the one-phase problem constructed from Cartan isoparametric cubic polynomials, which exist only in dimensions d=5,8,14,26, examining their stability and minimization properties.

Result: The solutions are unstable in dimensions d=5,8,14, and minimizing in dimension d=26.

Conclusion: Provides the first example of a singular minimizing cone in the Alt-Caffarelli problem that is neither axially symmetric nor of Lawson-type.

Abstract: In this paper we study a class of 1-homogeneous solutions of the one-phase problem coming from the Cartan isoparametric cubic polynomials, which exist only in dimensions $d=5,8,14,26$. Our main result shows that they are unstable in dimensions $d=5,8,14$, and minimizing in dimension $d=26$. This provides the first example of a singular minimizing cone in the Alt-Caffarelli problem that is neither axially symmetric nor of Lawson-type.

</details>


### [110] [Enhanced dissipation and stability threshold for the Navier--Stokes equations near Poiseuille flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35185)
*Tao Liang,Cuili Zhai,Xiaoping Zhai*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves nonlinear stability of the quadratic plane Poiseuille flow $(y^2,0)$ for 2D incompressible Navier-Stokes on $\mathbb T\times\mathbb R$, showing global stability for perturbations of size $O(\nu^{2/3})$ in a weighted Sobolev space, with enhanced dissipation of nonzero streamwise modes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding stability thresholds for shear flows is central in fluid dynamics. Quadratic (parabolic) Poiseuille flow poses challenges due to its non-monotonic/degenerate structure, and determining the sharp stability scale—and whether logarithmic losses can be avoided—is of interest.

Method: Analysis of the 2D incompressible Navier-Stokes equations linearized around $U(y)=(y^2,0)$ on $\mathbb T\times\mathbb R$, using a $\nu$-dependent weighted Sobolev space and techniques to capture enhanced dissipation of nonzero streamwise modes via the inviscid damping/linear mechanism.

Result: For initial perturbations of size $O(\nu^{2/3})$ in the weighted Sobolev space, the flow is globally nonlinearly stable. Nonzero streamwise modes exhibit enhanced dissipation, decaying exponentially on timescale $O(\nu^{-1/2})$, while the zero mode stays uniformly controlled.

Conclusion: In this weighted Sobolev topology, $O(\nu^{2/3})$ is a sufficient stability scale without logarithmic loss, establishing a clean nonlinear stability threshold for quadratic Poiseuille flow.

Abstract: We study the nonlinear stability of the quadratic plane Poiseuille flow $U(y)=(y^2,0)$ for the two-dimensional incompressible Navier--Stokes equations on $\mathbb T\times\mathbb R$. We prove that for initial perturbations of size $O(ν^{2/3})$ in a $ν$-dependent weighted Sobolev space, the flow is globally nonlinearly stable. In particular, the nonzero streamwise modes experience enhanced dissipation and decay exponentially on the characteristic time scale $O(ν^{-1/2})$, while the zero mode remains uniformly controlled. Thus, in this weighted Sobolev topology, \(O(ν^{2/3})\) is a sufficient stability scale without logarithmic loss.

</details>


### [111] [Convex counterexamples to the Schiffer and Pompeiu conjectures in dimensions three, four, six, eight, ten and fourteen](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35419)
*Jizhou Guo*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper constructs bounded convex, non-ball domains with real-analytic boundaries in dimensions 3, 4, 6, 8, 10, and 14 that admit nonconstant Helmholtz eigenfunctions (Δu+u=0) with u=1 and ∇u=0 on the boundary, thereby disproving the Schiffer and Pompeiu conjectures even within the convex class in these dimensions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Schiffer and Pompeiu conjectures predict that certain overdetermined eigenvalue/indicator conditions force the domain to be a ball. The authors seek counterexamples, particularly convex ones, which were previously unknown in any dimension.

Method: Construction via lifting conformal maps of meridian sections (dimension 3, axisymmetric O(2)-invariant) and via domains lying in rank-two compact Lie algebras 𝔲(2), 𝔰𝔬(4), 𝔰𝔲(3), 𝔰𝔬(5), 𝔤₂ for n=2m+2. Harish-Chandra's radial part formula reduces to planar Helmholtz problems and Kostant's theorem reduces convexity to the Cartan section. Exact solutions found by computer-assisted contraction in weighted polynomial coefficient spaces with interval bounds for finite blocks and analytic tail bounds.

Result: Bounded convex non-ball domains in dimensions 3,4,6,8,10,14 with real-analytic spherical boundaries admitting the desired eigenfunction; indicator Fourier transforms vanish on the unit sphere, disproving Schiffer and Pompeiu conjectures in the convex class. A strictly star-shaped but nonconvex example in dimension 3 is also given.

Conclusion: These are the first counterexamples to the Schiffer and Pompeiu conjectures in dimensions at least three, and the first known convex counterexamples in any dimension (aside from an earlier version of this work).

Abstract: In dimensions $3,4,6,8,10,14$ we construct bounded convex non-ball domains with real-analytic spherical boundaries. Each admits a nonconstant solution of $Δu+u=0$ with $u=1$ and $\nabla u=0$ on the boundary. Green's identity makes their indicator Fourier transforms vanish on the unit sphere, disproving the Schiffer and Pompeiu conjectures even within the convex class in these dimensions. The three-dimensional domain is axisymmetric, invariant under $O(2)$ on the last two coordinates and reflection of the first, and is obtained by lifting a conformal map of its meridian section. In dimensions $n=2m+2$, $m=1,2,3,4,6$, the domains lie in the rank-two compact Lie algebras $\mathfrak{u}(2)$, $\mathfrak{so}(4)$, $\mathfrak{su}(3)$, $\mathfrak{so}(5)$ and $\mathfrak g_2$. Harish-Chandra's radial part formula reduces these cases to planar Helmholtz problems, and Kostant's theorem reduces convexity to the Cartan section. Exact solutions near dyadic approximations follow from computer-assisted contractions in weighted polynomial coefficient spaces, with interval bounds for finite blocks and analytic bounds for every infinite tail. A second, strictly star-shaped but nonconvex, example in dimension three is retained as an independent result. To our knowledge, apart from the earlier version of this work, these are the first counterexamples in dimensions at least three and the first known convex counterexamples in any dimension.

</details>


### [112] [Global Regularity for the Chemotaxis-Stokes System with Signal Consumption in $\mathbb{R}^3$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35204)
*Fengqiang Shi,Wendong Wang,Guoxu Yang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves global classical solvability of the 3D chemotaxis--Stokes Cauchy problem for large data, resolving a longstanding regularity question for the Tuval et al. model with bilinear consumption.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Since Tuval et al. (2005) introduced the continuum model of aerobic bacteria, oxygen, and incompressible fluid, global classical regularity for large data in the 3D chemotaxis-consumption system remained open, even without the nonlinear convection term.

Method: A localized entropy estimate uniform under density normalization is the key new ingredient, valid even when the consumption coefficient becomes arbitrarily large. A terminal representation of the signal separates two mechanisms: consumption excludes normalized density concentration at positive-signal points, and a localized weighted estimate improves density integrability at zero-signal points.

Result: Under smooth, bounded, nonnegative initial density and signal with finite density mass, second moment, and signal Fisher energy, and divergence-free initial velocity in H^4, the chemotaxis--Stokes Cauchy problem in R^3 is globally classically solvable with no smallness assumption and with positive signal background allowed.

Conclusion: The combined mechanisms yield decay of a scale-invariant density quantity, enabling a local epsilon-regularity criterion to apply without a separate smallness assumption on the signal gradient, and the solution stays bounded on every finite time interval.

Abstract: In 2005, Tuval et al. introduced a continuum model for the interaction of aerobic bacteria, oxygen, and an incompressible fluid. For the associated three-dimensional chemotaxis-consumption systems, global classical regularity for large data has remained a longstanding question, even after omitting the nonlinear convection term from the fluid equation. We answer this question and prove global classical solvability of the chemotaxis--Stokes Cauchy problem in \(\mathbb{R}^3\), with linear cell diffusion and bilinear signal consumption, under the following data assumptions. The initial density and signal are smooth, bounded, and nonnegative, with finite density mass and second moment and finite signal Fisher energy; the divergence-free initial velocity belongs to \(H^4\). No smallness assumption is imposed, and a positive constant signal background is allowed. The solution remains bounded on every finite time interval. The main new ingredient is a localized entropy estimate uniform under density normalization, even when the resulting consumption coefficient becomes arbitrarily large. A terminal representation of the signal separates two complementary mechanisms: consumption excludes normalized density concentration at positive-signal points, while a localized weighted estimate improves density integrability at zero-signal points. Together, these mechanisms yield decay of a scale-invariant density quantity and allow a local $\varepsilon$-regularity criterion to be applied without a separate smallness assumption on the signal gradient.

</details>


### [113] [Uniform null-controllability times for the Coron--Guerrero problems are $2$ and $2 + 2 \sqrt{2}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35355)
*Kai Koike,Vincent Laheurte*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper determines the exact uniform null-controllability time (in the vanishing viscosity limit) for the transport-diffusion equation on [0,L] with Dirichlet boundary control, for both signs of transport velocity, disproving a conjecture by Coron and Guerrero.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The uniform null-controllability time (the infimum of times keeping controllability cost bounded as viscosity ε→0) was only partially understood. Coron and Guerrero conjectured T_unif=L/M for positive speed, and Lissy had disproved a related conjecture for negative speed, but the exact threshold remained open.

Method: Lower bounds are constructed via adjoint solutions violating uniform observability below the thresholds, exploiting asymptotic structure of Blaschke products and model spaces tied to the adjoint spectrum's exponential family. Upper bounds are reduced to infinite-time observability inequalities via a boundary-to-interior map representation and Hilbert-Schmidt norm estimates.

Result: Exact uniform times: T_unif = 2L/M for M>0, and T_unif = (2+2√2)L/|M| for M<0.

Conclusion: The positive-speed result disproves the Coron-Guerrero conjecture (T_unif=L/M); the negative-speed result gives the exact threshold, refining Lissy's earlier disproof.

Abstract: We study the uniform null-controllability, in the vanishing viscosity limit, of the transport--diffusion equation $u_t + M u_x = \varepsilon u_{xx}$ on $[0,L]$ with a Dirichlet boundary control at $x=0$. Our results concern the uniform null-controllability time $T_{\mathrm{unif}}$, defined as the infimum of the times for which the null-controllability cost remains bounded as $\varepsilon \to 0$. We determine this time exactly for both signs of the transport velocity: $T_{\mathrm{unif}} = 2 L / M$ in the positive-speed case $M>0$, and $T_{\mathrm{unif}} = (2 + 2 \sqrt{2}) L / |M|$ in the negative-speed case $M<0$. The positive-speed result disproves the conjecture $T_{\mathrm{unif}}=L/M$ suggested by Coron and Guerrero. For negative speed, the conjectured threshold had already been disproved by Lissy. Our result determines the exact threshold. We establish the lower bounds by constructing adjoint solutions that violate uniform observability below the respective thresholds. In the positive-speed case, the construction exploits the asymptotic structure of the Blaschke products and model spaces associated with the exponential family generated by the adjoint spectrum. The same model-space structure is used to reduce the upper-bound problems for both signs of $M$ to infinite-time observability inequalities, which are proved through a representation of the boundary-to-interior map and estimates of its Hilbert--Schmidt norm.

</details>


### [114] [Sharp Harmonic-Mean Bounds for Neumann Eigenvalues on Riemannian Surfaces and Counterexamples to Geodesic Ball Optimality on Higher-Dimensional Spheres](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35360)
*Hongtao Hu,Meiqi Liu,Qiaoran Wu,Wenming Zou*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: For a bounded simply connected Lipschitz domain with conformal metric of bounded positive Gaussian curvature (KM<4π), the reciprocal sum of the second and third Neumann eigenvalues is bounded below, with equality iff the interior is isometric to the constant-curvature geodesic disk.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: This work completes Conjecture 1.2 of Langford--Laugesen concerning Neumann eigenvalues in conformal metrics; characterizing the model disk that maximizes the first positive Neumann eigenvalue under curvature constraints.

Method: Neumann eigenvalue analysis on a bounded simply connected Lipschitz domain Ω with conformal metric g=ω|dz|², where ω>0 in the interior and may vanish at the boundary, assuming Gaussian curvature K_g≤K>0 and KM<4π.

Result: Proves 1/μ₂(Ω;ω)+1/μ₃(Ω;ω) ≥ 2/μ₂(D_K(M)), where D_K(M) is the geodesic disk of area M in the constant-curvature K surface; equality holds exactly when the interiors are isometric.

Conclusion: The conjecture is resolved: the constant-curvature model disk maximizes the first positive Neumann eigenvalue in this class of conformal metrics.

Abstract: Let $Ω\subset\mathbb C$ be a bounded simply connected Lipschitz domain with conformal metric $g=ω|dz|^2$, where $ω\in C^2(Ω)\cap L^\infty(Ω)$ is positive in the interior and may tend to zero at the boundary. If the Gaussian curvature satisfies $K_g\leq K$, $K>0$, and $KM<4π$, where $M=\int_Ωω\,dA$, then the Neumann eigenvalues, numbered by $0=μ_1(Ω;ω)<μ_2(Ω;ω)\leqμ_3(Ω;ω)\leq\cdots$, satisfy $\frac{1}{μ_2(Ω;ω)}+\frac{1}{μ_3(Ω;ω)}\geq\frac{2}{μ_2(D_K(M))}$. Here $D_K(M)$ is the geodesic disk of area $M$ in the surface of constant curvature $K$. We prove that equality holds exactly when the interiors are isometric. This completes Conjecture 1.2 of Langford--Laugesen; in particular, the model disk maximizes the first positive Neumann eigenvalue in this class.

</details>


### [115] [Local stability of direct Mach configurations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35365)
*Jun Chen,Xuemei Deng*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves local stability of 2D steady compressible Euler Mach configurations (incident/reflected shocks, Mach stem, slip line) with subsonic downstream states under a transversality condition and a truncation-segment condition, establishing existence, closeness, and uniqueness of perturbed Mach configurations via Lagrangian coordinates and an elliptic reduction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Mach configurations are fundamental shock-reflection patterns in 2D steady compressible flow, but their local structural stability under small perturbations of the incoming flow is a delicate free-boundary problem due to the unknown slip line/contact discontinuity. The paper aims to rigorously establish that such configurations persist and remain unique under small perturbations.

Method: Lagrangian coordinates transform the unknown contact discontinuity curve into a fixed boundary and reduce the full Euler system to a first-order elliptic system for flow direction and pressure. The contact discontinuity is handled by solving a mixed boundary value problem in divergence form with discontinuous coefficients for a single elliptic equation, so the contact conditions become compatibility conditions.

Result: Under a transversality condition on the shock polar loops and a suitable truncation-segment condition, every sufficiently small perturbation of the incoming flow yields a Mach configuration close to the background one (reflected shock, Mach stem, slip line, and downstream subsonic flow). The solution is unique in the class satisfying the a priori estimate (for incoming flows small in a slightly stronger norm).

Conclusion: The local stability and uniqueness of 2D steady compressible Euler Mach configurations with subsonic downstream states is established, providing a rigorous free-boundary elliptic framework that naturally preserves contact discontinuity conditions as compatibility conditions.

Abstract: We investigate the local stability of direct Mach configurations for the two-dimensional steady compressible Euler equations. Given a piecewise constant Mach configuration that consists of an incident shock, a reflected shock, a Mach stem and a slip line, with subsonic downstream states, we prove the following: under a transversality condition on the shock polar loops and a suitable condition on the truncation segment, every sufficiently small perturbation of the incoming flow gives rise to a Mach configuration that is a small perturbation of the background one, in the sense that the reflected shock, the Mach stem and the slip line, together with the downstream subsonic flow, stay close to their background counterparts; moreover, the solution is unique in the class of solutions satisfying the a priori estimate (for incoming flows that are small in a slightly stronger norm). Lagrangian coordinates are employed to transform the unknown contact discontinuity curve into a fixed boundary and to reduce the full Euler system to a first-order elliptic system for the flow direction and the pressure. The key idea in dealing with the contact discontinuity is to solve a mixed boundary value problem, in divergence form with discontinuous coefficients, for a single elliptic equation, so that the contact discontinuity conditions are naturally preserved as compatibility conditions for the solutions of the elliptic problem.

</details>


### [116] [Global minimality of the degree-one Ginzburg-Landau vortex solution in the unit ball in dimensions $n \geq 2$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35398)
*Radu Ignat,Luc Nguyen*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: For the Ginzburg-Landau functional with vortex boundary condition u(x)=x on ∂B, the paper proves that for every ε>0 there is a unique minimizer, given exactly by the rotationally symmetric vortex solution in dimension n≥2.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding minimizers of the Ginzburg-Landau functional with vortex boundary conditions is central in the analysis of vortices, and uniqueness/symmetry of minimizers is a subtle and important question.

Method: Variational analysis of the Ginzburg-Landau energy over R^n-valued maps on the unit ball B with boundary condition u(x)=x on ∂B, establishing existence and uniqueness of the minimizer.

Result: For every Ginzburg-Landau parameter ε>0, a unique minimizer exists and equals the rotationally symmetric vortex solution in dimensions n≥2.

Conclusion: The rotationally symmetric vortex is the exact unique minimizer for all ε>0 and all dimensions n≥2.

Abstract: We consider the problem of minimizing the Ginzburg-Landau functional among $\mathbb{R}^n$-valued maps from the unit ball $B \subset \mathbb{R}^n$ with vortex boundary condition $u(x) = x$ on $\partial B$. We show that, for every Ginzburg-Landau parameter $\varepsilon > 0$, there exists a unique minimizer given exactly by the rotationally symmetric vortex solution in dimension $n\geq 2$.

</details>


### [117] [Finite-Time Blowup for Navier-Stokes with Smooth Forcing, Part I: Construction of Self-Similar Solutions with Admissible Stress and Flat Remainder](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35406)
*Zhen Lei,Xiao Ren*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: A readable exposition of the profile-construction part of OpenAI's manuscript on finite-time blowup for Navier-Stokes, presenting smooth axi-symmetric profiles whose residual has a divergence-form term plus a rapidly vanishing remainder, with stress/radial shear satisfying an admissible cone condition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To make accessible the profile-construction used in OpenAI's claimed advance on the Navier-Stokes Millennium Prize Problem, and to clarify the inner-core construction via a new linear model.

Method: Constructs smooth axi-symmetric profiles; when inserted into Navier-Stokes they yield a residual comprising a divergence-form term and a remainder vanishing to infinite order in 1-t on fixed similarity sectors. A new linear model is introduced to better explain the inner core; the stress/radial shear satisfy the admissible cone condition (stress need not be small). Oscillatory-pulse cancellation is deferred to a companion paper.

Result: Profiles are smooth and axi-symmetric; residual decomposes into a divergence-form term plus a remainder vanishing to infinite order in 1-t on similarity sectors; stress and radial shear satisfy the admissible cone condition.

Conclusion: The profile-construction portion of OpenAI's blowup manuscript is reformulated accessibly, with a new linear model clarifying the inner core; the remaining residual correction via oscillatory pulses is handled in the companion paper.

Abstract: This paper gives a readable and accessible version of the profile-construction part of OpenAI's manuscript "Finite Time Blowup for Navier-Stokes". We regard OpenAI's work as a major advance on the Navier-Stokes Millennium Prize Problem. The profiles are smooth and axi-symmetric. Inserting them into the Navier-Stokes equations gives a residual consisting of a divergence-form term and a remainder vanishing to infinite order in $1-t$ on fixed similarity sectors. The associated stress and radial shear satisfy the admissible cone condition. The stress need not be small. We introduce a new linear model that provides a clearer explanation of the inner core construction. The cancellation by oscillatory pulses will be treated in the companion paper "Finite-Time Blowup for Navier-Stokes with Smooth Forcing, Part II: Residual Correction via Oscillatory Pulses".

</details>


### [118] [Interacting fronts in the strongly nonlocal Allen--Cahn equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35666)
*Erisa Hasani,Stefania Patrizi*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Studies the sharp-interface limit of the fractional Allen-Cahn equation in R^n (n>=2) for s in (0,1/2), with finitely many nested transition layers, deriving a coupled geometric motion law for the fronts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The fractional Allen-Cahn equation's sharp-interface limit is well understood in the critical regime s=1/2 (independent front motion), but the strongly nonlocal regime s<1/2 remains less understood, where nonlocal interactions between fronts are expected to matter.

Method: Analyze the sharp-interface limit for s in (0,1/2) with initial data of finitely many nested transition layers. Derive a coupled geometric law where each front's velocity includes fractional mean curvature plus a nonlocal interaction potential generated by the other fronts. Assume a smooth strictly-nested evolution exists, then prove local uniform convergence to integer-valued phases away from moving fronts.

Result: A coupled geometric motion law is identified: each front's velocity contains fractional mean curvature and a nonlocal interaction potential from the other fronts. These interactions persist while fronts remain separated, unlike the independent motion at s=1/2.

Conclusion: Under the assumption that the coupled law admits a smooth evolution with strictly nested sets on a time interval, the Allen-Cahn solutions converge locally uniformly away from the moving fronts to the corresponding integer-valued phases.

Abstract: We study the sharp-interface limit of the fractional Allen--Cahn equation in $\R^n$, $n\geq 2$, in the strongly nonlocal regime $s\in(0,\frac12)$, for initial data consisting of finitely many nested transition layers. We identify a coupled geometric law for the motion of the resulting fronts: each velocity contains fractional mean curvature and a nonlocal interaction potential generated by the other fronts. These interactions persist while the fronts remain separated, in contrast to the independent motion in the critical regime $s=\frac12$. Assuming that the coupled law admits a smooth evolution with strictly nested sets on a given time interval, we prove that the Allen--Cahn solutions converge locally uniformly away from the moving fronts to the corresponding integer-valued phases.

</details>


### [119] [On the classification of solutions to the Logarithmic Laplacian critical Choquard equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35669)
*Rakesh Arora,Jacques Giacomoni*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Establishes a sharp logarithmic Choquard inequality and classifies positive classical solutions of a critical logarithmic Choquard problem involving the logarithmic Laplacian, showing they are explicit bubbles in a suitable integrability class.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Sharp logarithmic Choquard inequalities and the classification of solutions to the associated critical logarithmic Choquard problem are not yet established; this work fills that gap by connecting entropy and Pitt-type inequalities and by understanding logarithmic Laplacian equations.

Method: Combines Beckner's entropy inequality with the sharp Pitt-type inequality to prove the logarithmic Choquard inequality; classifies positive classical solutions in a suitable integrability class; constructs explicit solutions as limits of critical fractional Choquard bubbles.

Result: Proves the sharp logarithmic Choquard inequality and shows every positive classical solution in the prescribed class has the explicit form u_{σ,t}(x)=e^{(N/4)(σ-B_{N,L})} B_{N,0}(t/(t²+|x-x_0|²))^{N/2}, with constants B_{N,0} and B_{N,L} given explicitly.

Conclusion: The paper provides a sharp inequality and a complete classification of positive classical solutions to the critical logarithmic Choquard problem via explicit bubble profiles, extending the Choquard theory to the logarithmic Laplacian setting.

Abstract: In this work, we establish a sharp logarithmic Choquard inequality by combining Beckner's entropy inequality with the sharp Pitt-type inequality. Motivated by this estimate, we classify the positive classical solutions, in a suitable integrability class, of the critical logarithmic Choquard problem \[ \mathcal{L}_Δu = σu + \frac{1}{\|u\|_2^2} \left(G_{\ln}(u)-\frac{4}{N}\int_{\mathbb{R}^N}u^2\ln u\,dx\right)u \qquad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^N, \] where $σ\in\mathbb{R}$, $N\geq 1$, $\mathcal{L}_Δ$ is the logarithmic Laplacian, and $G_{\ln}(u)=\ln(1/|x|^4)*u^2$. We construct explicit solutions as limits of critical fractional Choquard bubbles and prove that every positive classical solution in the prescribed class has the form \[ u_{σ,t}(x) = e^{\frac{N}{4}(σ-B_{N,\mathcal{L}})} B_{N,0}\left(\frac{t}{t^2+|x-x_0|^2}\right)^{N/2}, \qquad t>0,\quad x_0\in\mathbb{R}^N, \] where \[ B_{N,0} =\left(\frac{Γ(N)}{π^{N/2}Γ(N/2)}\right)^{1/2}, \qquad B_{N,\mathcal{L}} =2\ln 2+4ψ(N/2)-2ψ(N)+\frac{4}{N}\ln B_{N,0}. \]

</details>


<div id='physics.comp-ph'></div>

# physics.comp-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [120] [Uncertainty Quantification for the Fission Matrix Method: A Rigorous Mathematical Framework and Computationally Efficient Alternatives](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33161)
*Valerio Mascolino*

Main category: physics.comp-ph

TL;DR: This work develops a full-covariance Delta-method framework to propagate Monte Carlo statistical uncertainty to the fission matrix (FM) keff and fission source, benchmarked against diagonal and resampling estimators on the JSI TRIGA Mark-II reactor.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior uncertainty propagation in FM methods assumes independent coefficients without testing this against their actual covariance. This assumption is rarely validated, and the true covariance structure of Monte Carlo-derived FM coefficients has not been rigorously characterized.

Method: A full-covariance Delta-method framework is derived for propagating uncertainty to keff and the fission source, evaluated alongside a diagonal-covariance approximation and direct resampling of FM coefficients, using an OpenMC model of the JSI TRIGA Mark-II reactor (including all-rods-out and FM-CRd control-rod cases).

Result: For all-rods-out, the three estimators and a 100-run empirical benchmark agree within 5.7% for keff and 1% for the fission source; the diagonal bias sign flips between statistics. With global fixed-source FM generation, diagonal, resampling, and independent-run estimators agree within ~2% and lie ~10% below the full-covariance estimate. Off-diagonal covariance is large and nearly symmetric, largely cancelling when propagated. For FM-CRd, keff matches the exact perturbed reference within 60 pcm (~2 sigma) and fission source within 0.7%, with diagonal and resampling estimators conservative by a factor of ~2.

Conclusion: Given the prohibitive memory cost of the full covariance, the diagonal Delta method is recommended as the practical default, with the full covariance reserved for one-time system characterization.

Abstract: The fission matrix (FM) method recasts neutron transport in matrix form, enabling fast, interpolation-based reactor calculations from a pre-computed database of Monte Carlo-derived coefficients. Propagation of the underlying Monte Carlo statistical uncertainty to the FM eigenvalue and eigenvector is rarely rigorous: prior approaches assume independent coefficients without testing this against their actual covariance. This work derives a full-covariance Delta-method framework for propagating uncertainty to keff and the fission source, and evaluates it with two cheaper alternatives, a diagonal-covariance approximation and direct resampling of the FM coefficients, using an OpenMC model of the JSI TRIGA Mark-II reactor. For the all-rods-out case, these three estimators and an empirical benchmark of 100 independent Monte Carlo runs agree within 5.7% for keff and 1% for the fission source. The sign of the diagonal approximation's bias flips between the two statistics levels, so it must be checked case by case. With a global fixed-source FM generation strategy, the diagonal, resampling, and independent-run estimators agree within about 2% and lie roughly 10% below the full-covariance estimate, a gap attributed to covariance sampling noise. The large off-diagonal covariance is nearly symmetric and mostly cancels when propagated, rather than being small or negative as previously assumed. For the FM control-rod interpolation and combination strategy (FM-CRd), keff matches an exact perturbed reference within 60 pcm (about two standard deviations) and the fission source within 0.7%, while the diagonal and resampling estimators agree closely and are conservative by about a factor of two. Given this and the prohibitive memory cost of the full covariance, the diagonal Delta method is recommended as the practical default, with the full covariance reserved for one-time characterization of a system.

</details>


### [121] [When does the moment basis matter in lattice Boltzmann methods?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33328)
*Alessandro De Rosis*

Main category: physics.comp-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes when orthogonalizing the moment basis changes a central-moment lattice Boltzmann scheme, showing the outcome depends on velocity-set geometry and moment relaxation rates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Orthogonalization of the moment basis is a common choice in lattice Boltzmann methods, but it is unclear whether and when it actually alters the collision scheme. The authors aim to determine the conditions under which orthogonalizing the basis leaves the scheme invariant versus changes it.

Method: The collision depends on the basis only via the conjugate T^{-1}ΛT. Two bases related by a constant matrix A give the same scheme iff [Λ,A]_{ij}=A_{ij}(λ_i−λ_j) vanishes, and on physical states only columns for non-conserved moments matter. The authors analyze lattices with velocities in {0,±1}^d and symmetric weights, plus rectangular lattices.

Result: On D2Q9 and D3Q15 the shear rate remains free regardless of orthogonalization; on D3Q19 and D3Q27 the scheme changes whenever fourth-order moments do not relax at the shear rate. On rectangular lattices, an independent bulk rate yields an orthogonal counterpart that does not share the published scheme's transport coefficients. The rest-equilibrium-orthogonal basis was never less robust by more than a few percent and was often more robust, and more accurate against Taylor-Green DNS, costing at most 2% more on D3Q27.
