Conclusion: Whether orthogonalizing the moment basis changes a central-moment lattice Boltzmann scheme is governed by velocity-set geometry. Orthogonalization reaches shear moments only through face and body diagonals, and a rest-equilibrium-orthogonal basis offers stability and accuracy benefits at modest computational cost.

Abstract: Whether orthogonalising the moment basis changes a central-moment lattice Boltzmann scheme is decided by the geometry of the velocity set. The basis enters the collision only through the conjugate $\mathit{T}^{-1} Λ\mathit{T}$, so two bases related by a constant matrix $\mathit{A}$ define exactly the same scheme if and only if $[Λ,\mathit{A}]_{ij}=\mathit{A}_{ij}(λ_i-λ_j)$ vanishes, and on physical states only its columns for the non-conserved moments matter. We prove that on lattices with velocities in $\{0,\pm1\}^d$ and symmetric weights orthogonalisation reaches the shear moments only through the face and body diagonals: whatever the orthogonalisation, the shear rate is free on D2Q9 and D3Q15, whereas on D3Q19 and D3Q27 the scheme changes whenever the fourth-order moments do not relax at the shear rate. On rectangular lattices, where the symmetry is broken, an independent bulk rate yields an orthogonal counterpart that does not share the transport coefficients of the published scheme, which is itself orthogonal in the inner product of the rest equilibrium up to the one relaxation entry that consistency requires. Where the formulations differ but share their hydrodynamics, a basis orthogonal in the inner product of the rest equilibrium, which guarantees linear stability at rest, was never less robust in the least stable flow direction by more than a few per cent, often much more robust, and the more accurate against a Taylor-Green DNS. Computed through the non-orthogonal transforms with a conjugated relaxation matrix, it costs at most $2\%$ more on D3Q27.

</details>


### [122] [Stochastic Physics-Constrained Operator Inference for Complex Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33380)
*Changhong Mou*

Main category: physics.comp-ph

TL;DR: SPOpInf learns stochastic quadratic-linear drift and diffusion amplitude from data via EM, enforcing energy-preserving structure and removing non-identifiability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Quadratically nonlinear systems with additive noise appear across science and engineering as stochastic dynamical systems and as ROMs of turbulent flows. Existing operator inference (OpInf) fits only a deterministic drift by least squares, ignoring the stochastic component and failing to jointly calibrate drift and noise.

Method: SPOpInf is a non-intrusive estimator that maximizes an Euler-Maruyama increment likelihood with unknown residual covariance, jointly calibrating deterministic and stochastic parts. It enforces energy-preserving quadratic structure via linear equality constraints inside the optimization, uses an upper-triangular parametrization to remove structural non-identifiability, and solves the problem with an EM-based iterative scheme alternating a weighted least-squares drift update and a residual-variance noise update.

Result: On five stochastic benchmarks exhibiting intermittency and chaos, SPOpInf recovers deterministic operators more accurately than OpInf, satisfies constraints to machine precision, estimates noise amplitudes accurately, and reproduces long-time statistics. For a stochastic Burgers equation and a quasi-geostrophic double-gyre flow, it yields non-intrusive stochastic ROMs stable over long times where unconstrained alternatives blow up.

Conclusion: SPOpInf provides an accurate, structure-preserving, and stable non-intrusive approach for learning stochastic ROMs of quadratically nonlinear noisy systems.

Abstract: Quadratically nonlinear systems driven by additive noise arise throughout science and engineering, both as stochastic dynamical systems in their own right and as reduced-order models (ROMs) of turbulent flows. We propose stochastic physics-constrained operator inference (SPOpInf), a non-intrusive method that learns a stochastic differential equation, i.e., a quadratic--linear drift and a diffusion amplitude, from data. Unlike operator inference (OpInf), which fits a deterministic drift by least squares, SPOpInf maximizes an Euler--Maruyama increment likelihood in which the residual covariance is itself an unknown, so the deterministic and stochastic components are calibrated jointly; it enforces the energy-preserving quadratic structure exactly, through linear equality constraints imposed inside the optimization; and it works in an upper-triangular parametrization that removes the structural non-identifiability of the full quadratic tensor. The resulting optimization is solved by an expectation--maximization (EM)-based iterative learning algorithm that updates the drift coefficients and the noise levels in turn: each iteration requires only an inexpensive weighted least-squares solve for the drift and a residual-variance update for the noise. With five stochastic benchmark systems that show intermittency and chaos, SPOpInf recovers the deterministic operators more accurately than OpInf, satisfies the imposed constraints to machine precision, accurately estimates the noise amplitudes, and reproduces long-time statistics. Applied to a stochastic Burgers equation and a quasi-geostrophic double-gyre flow, the same estimator yields non-intrusive stochastic ROMs that remain stable over long times where unconstrained alternatives blow up.

</details>


<div id='physics.plasm-ph'></div>

# physics.plasm-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [123] [Joint Amplitude-Phase Optimization of Broadband Lasers Raises Absolute Two-Plasmon-Decay Thresholds Above Coherence-Time Predictions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31942)
*A. S. Joglekar,R. K. Follett,D. H. Froula,J. P. Palastro*

Main category: physics.plasm-ph

TL;DR: At fixed power spectrum, optimizing spectral phase raises the two-plasmon-decay (TPD) threshold, but joint amplitude-phase optimization outperforms transform-limited pulse trains while keeping peak-to-average ratio low.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Broadband lasers suppress the two-plasmon-decay instability that preheats inertial-fusion targets, but the conventional coherence-time scaling ignores spectral phase. The paper seeks a better control variable for suppressing TPD.

Method: Gradient descent through a differentiable enveloped wave solver to optimize spectral phase and joint amplitude-phase of the laser drive; an exact growth-rate budget separates suppression mechanisms.

Result: Phase optimization raises threshold from random-phase median 2.8 I_mono to 4.1 I_mono, but yields TL pulse train with R=32. Joint amplitude-phase optimization reaches 5.6 I_mono at R=3.6 (vs. best TL 4.5 I_mono at R=16); relaxing peak constraint raises threshold to 6.4 I_mono. Advantage persists from 1%-4% bandwidth and OMEGA to ignition scales.

Conclusion: TL suppresses coupling by concentrating pump into short bursts, lowering time-averaged magnitude. Joint optima retain near-random amplitude while suppressing available coupling and pump-daughter alignment, offering superior TPD suppression.

Abstract: Broadband lasers suppress the absolute two-plasmon-decay instability that preheats inertial-fusion targets. The usual scaling relates this suppression to the drive's coherence time, a power-spectrum statistic insensitive to spectral phase. By gradient descent through a differentiable enveloped wave solver, we show that at fixed power spectrum phase optimization raises the threshold from the random-phase median of $2.8\,I_\mathrm{mono}$ to $4.1\,I_\mathrm{mono}$, but produces a transform-limited (TL) pulse train with peak-to-average ratio $R=32$. Joint amplitude--phase optimization reaches $5.6\,I_\mathrm{mono}$ at $R=3.6$, above the best tested TL line-count result of $4.5\,I_\mathrm{mono}$ at $R=16$; relaxing the peak constraint raises the joint threshold to $6.4\,I_\mathrm{mono}$. An exact growth-rate budget evaluated inside the simulation separates the two mechanisms. TL suppresses coupling mainly by concentrating the pump field into short bursts, thereby lowering its time-averaged magnitude at fixed average intensity. The joint optima retain near-random values of this amplitude measure while suppressing both the available coupling and the fraction realized through pump--daughter alignment. The advantage of joint optimization persists from $1\%$ to $4\%$ bandwidth and from OMEGA-scale to ignition-scale conditions.

</details>


### [124] [Controlling $γ$-photon production with additively manufactured microstructured targets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32070)
*Joshua Luoma,Andreas Kemp,Thomas G. White,Dean Rusby,Hui Chen,Gennady Shvets*

Main category: physics.plasm-ph

TL;DR: PIC simulations explore microstructured targets, compatible with two-photon polymerization (TPP) 3D printing, to optimize nonlinear inverse Compton scattering (NICS) for generating high-flux MeV γ-photon sources.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: NICS in high-density targets can produce high-flux, broadband MeV γ-photons useful for radiography, photonuclear physics, and pair production. Microstructured targets offer control over photon energy, angular distribution, and yield, but optimal designs compatible with scalable fabrication (TPP) need investigation.

Method: Particle-in-cell (PIC) simulations of NICS with microstructured targets that are compatible with two-photon polymerization additive manufacturing (features down to ~200 nm). Simulation analysis is combined with simple analytical models to guide target design.

Result: Achieved optimized designs yielding 2.0·10^8 photons/J exceeding 100 MeV (low-divergence, high-energy beams) and upwards of 2.0·10^11 photons/J above 1 MeV (high yield) in the multi-PW regime.

Conclusion: Microstructured targets compatible with TPP printing enable control over the NICS process, and combined PIC-analytical approaches establish pathways to optimize targets for high-energy, low-divergence and high-yield γ-photon sources.

Abstract: Nonlinear inverse Compton scattering (NICS) in high-density targets offers a promising route for generating high flux, broadband sources of MeV $γ$-photons which are important for applications like radiography, photonuclear physics, and pair production. Microstructured targets enable control over the NICS process to help shape the photon energy, angular distribution, and total yield. This work presents particle-in-cell (PIC) simulations exploring target microstructures compatible with two-photon polymerization (TPP) additive manufacturing, which is a promising technique that allows high-volume and reproducible printing of sub-wavelength features as small as 200 nm. We investigate how microstructures influence NICS radiation and merge simulation analysis with simple analytical models that guide target design. These results establish pathways to optimize targets for low-divergence, high-energy photon beams with 2.0$\cdot$10$^{8}$ photons/J exceeding 100 MeV and targets for high yields delivering upwards of 2.0$\cdot$10$^{11}$ photons/J above 1 MeV in the multi-PW regime.

</details>


### [125] [Review of LHD-ECRH activities and beyond](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32265)
*Masaki Nishiura,Hiroe Igami,Hiromi Takahashi,Naoki Kenmochi,Ryoma Yanai,Kenji Ueda,Yoshinori Mizuno,Toshiki Takeuchi,Yasuo Yoshimura,Takashi Shimozuma,Shin Kubo,Toru Ii Tsujimura,Sakuji Kobayashi,Tsuyoshi Kariya*

Main category: physics.plasm-ph

TL;DR: Historical review of the ECRH system on LHD (1998–2025), covering hardware advances, advanced plasma regimes, diagnostic and control uses, and transfer to next-generation helical devices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To document nearly three decades of ECRH contributions on LHD and to show how the accumulated technology and operational expertise can be leveraged for future helical fusion devices.

Method: A historical review approach tracing the stepwise evolution of ECRH hardware (gyrotrons, transmission lines, launcher systems) and the plasma regimes, diagnostics, and control experiments it enabled.

Result: The ECRH system supported advances in overdense heating and long-pulse steady-state operation, and served as a high-power diagnostic probe (CTS, CECE) and a flexible actuator for transport studies and real-time control.

Conclusion: The technological assets and operational expertise from LHD's ECRH program are being carried forward to next-generation helical devices (CHD and CHD-U) and broader gyrotron and millimeter-wave applications.

Abstract: The electron cyclotron resonance heating (ECRH) system on the Large Helical Device (LHD) played a key role in the progress of helical fusion research from the first plasma in 1998 to the final discharge on December 25, 2025. This paper presents a historical review of ECRH activities on LHD, highlighting the stepwise advances in hardware, including gyrotrons, transmission lines, and launcher systems, together with the exploration of advanced plasma regimes such as overdense heating and long-pulse steady-state operation. Beyond plasma heating, the ECRH system was also used as a high-power probe for advanced diagnostics, including collective Thomson scattering (CTS) as well as correlation electron cyclotron emission (CECE), and as a flexible actuator for transport studies and real-time control experiments. Finally, we discuss how the technological assets and operational expertise accumulated over the nearly three decades of the LHD program are being further developed and applied to next-generation helical devices, particularly CHD and CHD-U, as well as to broader gyrotron and millimeter-wave applications.

</details>


### [126] [In-situ Generation of Polarized Attosecond Positron Bunches via Radiation-Wakefield Induced Two-Photon Pairs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33285)
*Zhen-Ke Dou,Qian Zhao,Hao-Tian Wang,Feng Wan,Jian-Xing Li*

Main category: physics.plasm-ph

TL;DR: A single-shot, source-free plasma method produces, traps, and accelerates dense, spin-polarized attosecond positron bunches, verified by spin-resolved QED-PIC simulations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: High-energy, dense, spin-polarized attosecond positron beams are highly desirable for advanced accelerator physics, laboratory astrophysics, and ultrafast matter-antimatter studies, but are beyond conventional source and injection-based plasma-acceleration schemes.

Method: An ultraintense hollow laser in a plasma channel drives a radiative wakefield; injected electrons emit MeV gamma rays via nonlinear Compton scattering, photon-photon collisions induce linear Breit-Wheeler pair production, and radiative processes transfer polarization. Spin-resolved QED particle-in-cell simulations model the process.

Result: The laser-modulated wakefield captures positrons born in the frontier bubble wake, forming collimated bunch trains with hundred-attosecond duration, GeV-level energies, densities up to 10^17 cm^-3, and maintained polarization. Parameter scans show robustness to laser intensity and plasma-channel density variations.

Conclusion: This integrated in-situ approach simultaneously addresses positron production, polarization, temporal compression, and plasma injection, offering a compact route toward polarized attosecond antimatter beams.

Abstract: High-energy, dense, spin-polarized positron beams with attosecond duration are highly desirable for advanced accelerator physics, laboratory astrophysics, and ultrafast matter-antimatter studies, yet remain beyond the capability of conventional source and injection-based plasma-acceleration schemes. We propose an integrated in-situ method for production, trapping, and accelerating polarized attosecond positron bunches in a single plasma stage. An ultraintense hollow laser propagating in a plasma channel drives a radiative wakefield, where longitudinally injected electrons emit a dense MeV gamma-ray bath through nonlinear Compton scattering. Subsequent photon-photon collisions induce linear Breit-Wheeler pair production, while linear and nonlinear radiative processes mediate polarization transfer to the produced positrons. Spin-resolved QED particle-in-cell simulations indicate that the laser-modulated wakefield naturally captures positrons born in the frontier bubble wake, forming collimated bunch trains with hundred-attosecond duration, GeV-level energies, densities up to 10^17 cm^-3, and maintained polarization. Parameter scans demonstrate robustness against variations of laser intensity and plasma-channel density. This single-shot, source-free approach simultaneously addresses positron production, polarization, temporal compression, and plasma injection, offering a compact route toward polarized attosecond antimatter beams.

</details>


### [127] [Ion-temperature-gradient turbulence from finite to weak magnetic shear regime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33341)
*Zihao Wang,Tiannan Wu,Shaojie Wang*

Main category: physics.plasm-ph

TL;DR: The radial width of ITG poloidal harmonics is constrained both by parallel Landau damping and by micro-turbulence isotropy perpendicular to the magnetic field. In the weak-shear limit, this geometric constraint yields a critical magnetic shear s_crit ≈ 1/(2π) separating extended Type II from localized Type I ballooning modes, confirmed by gyrokinetic simulations and linked to internal transport barrier formation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Turbulent transport driven by the ITG mode is a central obstacle to tokamak fusion. Weak- or zero-magnetic-shear scenarios (e.g., ITER hybrid scenario) achieve improved confinement, but the underlying mechanism has remained unclear. This work aims to explain why weak shear improves confinement.

Method: The authors analyze the radial width of ITG poloidal harmonics, identifying a constraint from the isotropy of micro-turbulence in the plane perpendicular to the magnetic field in addition to parallel Landau damping. They derive a geometric criterion (s_crit ≈ 1/(2π)) and test it with global gyrokinetic simulations spanning DIII-D, JET, and ITER parameters, including nonlinear simulations with sustained heating.

Result: In the weak-shear limit, micro-isotropy dominates and constrains the radial width at one poloidal wavelength, giving s_crit ≈ 1/(2π). Above this threshold, extended Type II ballooning modes prevail; below it, localized Type I modes emerge, comprising only two or three harmonics. Simulations across DIII-D, JET, and ITER confirm the criterion, and nonlinear simulations show weak-shear plasmas spontaneously form internal transport barriers where turbulence is suppressed in the region |s| < s_crit.

Conclusion: This geometric criterion defines the weak-magnetic-shear regime and offers a fresh perspective on turbulent transport in fusion plasmas, linking weak shear to internal transport barrier formation.

Abstract: The tokamak, a toroidal magnetic device confining a hot plasma, is one of the most advanced approaches to fusion energy. A central obstacle is turbulent transport driven by the ion-temperature-gradient (ITG) mode. Operation scenarios that rely on a weak or zero magnetic shear core, such as the ITER hybrid scenario, have achieved markedly improved confinement, yet the underlying mechanism has remained unclear. Here we show that the radial width of ITG poloidal harmonics is constrained not only by the familiar parallel Landau damping, but also by the isotropy of micro-turbulence in the plane perpendicular to the magnetic field. In the weak-shear limit, micro-isotropy dominates and constrains the radial width at one poloidal wavelength, yielding a critical magnetic shear $s_{\text{crit}} \approx 1/(2π)$. Above this threshold, the extended Type II ballooning modes prevail; below it, the localized Type I modes emerge, comprising only two or three harmonics. Global gyrokinetic simulations spanning DIII-D, JET~and ITER parameters confirm this geometric criterion. Nonlinear simulations with sustained heating show that weak-shear plasmas spontaneously form internal transport barriers with the turbulence suppressed in the radial region determined by $|s| < s_{\text{crit}}$. This geometric criterion defines the weak-magnetic-shear regime and offers a fresh perspective on turbulent transport in fusion plasmas.

</details>


### [128] [Continuous Laser-Driven α Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33483)
*Noam Popper,Griffin D. Glenn,Girik Jain,Raz Halifa Levi,Tomer Catabi,Michal Elkind,Aviv Levinson,Assaf Levanon,Siegfried H. Glenzer,Ishay Pomerantz*

Main category: physics.plasm-ph

TL;DR: A continuous laser-driven α-particle source using a replenishable ultrathin water-leaf jet target achieves stable proton-boron fusion over 18,000 consecutive shots at 10 Hz.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Solid-target laser-driven proton-boron fusion is limited by target degradation and low repetition rate, hindering practical continuous α-particle generation.

Method: Target-normal sheath acceleration from a continuously replenishable ultrathin water-leaf jet produces a proton beam that hits a downstream boron nitride converter, driving the ¹¹B(p,α)⁸Be reaction. Results benchmarked against particle-transport simulations.

Result: Stable α-particle production sustained over 18,000 shots at 10 Hz. A pronounced flattening appears in the energy-differential α spectrum, caused by depth-dependent reaction yield and stopping power effects.

Conclusion: The high cumulative, stable α-particle source enables applications in targeted particle therapy and laser-driven plasma-fusion research.

Abstract: Laser-driven proton-boron fusion offers a path for generation of $α$ particles on a compact scale. Conventional solid-target experiments, however, are fundamentally limited by target degradation and low shot-repetition capacity. Here, we demonstrate a continuous laser-driven $α$ particle source utilizing target-normal sheath acceleration from a continuously replenishable ultrathin water-leaf jet target. The proton beam interacts with a downstream boron nitride converter, driving the $^{11}\text{B}(p,α)^{8}\text{Be}$ reaction which yields three $α$ particles. The measurement was repeated across $18{,}000$ consecutive shots at a rate of $10\,\text{Hz}$. The outgoing $α$-particle yield and spectral fluence were measured and benchmarked against comprehensive particle-transport simulations. The numerical and experimental results reveal a pronounced flattening in the energy-differential $α$-particle spectrum escaping from the converter, driven by depth-dependent reaction yield and stopping power effects. The sustained stable $α$-particle production at high cumulative yields paves the way for applications in targeted particle therapy and laser-driven plasma-fusion research.

</details>


### [129] [A Rapid Integrated Tokamak Modelling Approach for Coupled Profile and Equilibrium Evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33500)
*Ruohan Zhang,Huasheng Xie,Feng Wang,Zheng-Xiong Wang*

Main category: physics.plasm-ph

TL;DR: A rapid integrated tokamak modelling approach couples a fixed-boundary Grad-Shafranov equilibrium with particle/energy flux matching and weak-form current diffusion, applied to an ITER-like deuterium ramp scenario, achieving high accuracy and 100x+ speedup over conventional solvers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: During plasma heating and current ramps, the thermal and magnetic states of a tokamak evolve on different timescales, requiring integrated modelling that is both accurate and computationally fast.

Method: Couples a variational fixed-boundary Grad-Shafranov equilibrium to sparse particle and energy flux matching and weak-form current diffusion. Applied to an ITER-like pure-deuterium scenario: 10-15 MA current ramp over 20 s, 20 s flat-top, 20 s ramp-down to 14 MA, with 80 MW electron-cyclotron heating at start.

Result: Pedestal-top toroidal current density more than doubles during ramp-up then decreases in flat-top as current redistributes inward. Thermal stored energy rises from 185.52 to 252.70 MJ, nearing final value within seconds. FUSE gives 0.02% stored-energy difference at 60 s; largest profile differences are in core current density. Halving coupling interval changes stored energy by at most 0.006%. The 60 s pulse (600 intervals of 0.1 s) runs in 8.62 s on an Apple M4 Pro, at least two orders of magnitude faster than conventional solvers.

Conclusion: The approach provides rapid, accurate integrated tokamak modelling, validated against FUSE and demonstrating two-orders-of-magnitude speedup, suitable for efficient simulation of plasma heating and current ramp scenarios.

Abstract: During plasma heating and current ramps the thermal and magnetic states of a tokamak evolve on different timescales. We present a rapid integrated tokamak modelling approach, which couples a variational fixed-boundary Grad--Shafranov equilibrium to sparse particle and energy flux matching and to weak-form current diffusion. An ITER-like pure-deuterium scenario is examined: a current ramp from 10 to 15~MA over 20~s, a 20~s flat-top and a 20~s ramp-down to 14~MA, under 80~MW of electron-cyclotron heating applied at the start. The toroidal current density at the pedestal top more than doubles during the ramp-up and then decreases during the flat-top as current redistributes inward. Thermal stored energy rises from 185.52 to 252.70~MJ and approaches its final value within the first few seconds. An independent calculation with the Fusion Synthesis Engine (FUSE) gives a stored-energy difference of 0.02\% at 60~s, whereas the largest profile differences occur in the core current density. Halving the coupling interval changes stored energy by at most 0.006\% at the selected radial resolution. The 60~s pulse, advanced through 600 coupling intervals of 0.1~s, takes 8.62~s on an Apple M4 Pro, which is at least two orders of magnitude faster than conventional integrated tokamak modelling solvers.

</details>


### [130] [Implicit-Explicit time integration scheme with Physics-based preconditioning for two-fluid tokamak boundary simulations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33677)
*Micol Bassanini,Simone Deparis,Paolo Ricci,Brenno De Lucca,Davide Mancini,Louis Stenger,Sergio García Herreros*

Main category: physics.plasm-ph

TL;DR: A globally stiffly accurate IMEX Runge-Kutta scheme is implemented in the GBS code for two-fluid plasma turbulence, treating shear Alfvén waves and parallel diffusion implicitly with a 3D physics-based preconditioner, enabling large time steps and better efficiency than adaptive explicit schemes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Explicit schemes face restrictive CFL limits from stiff phenomena like shear Alfvén waves and parallel diffusion, while fully implicit approaches incur high computational cost; a middle-ground splitting is needed for efficient two-fluid plasma turbulence simulations.

Method: A globally stiffly accurate IMEX Runge-Kutta scheme in the GBS code: stiff terms (shear Alfvén waves, parallel diffusion) treated implicitly, other terms explicitly. A 3D physics-based preconditioner, inspired by MHD techniques, efficiently solves the implicit subsystem each step. Verified via the method of manufactured solutions.

Result: Time steps well beyond the CFL limit are achieved. The framework shows algorithmic and parallel scalability, and demonstrates significant gains in numerical stability and computational efficiency versus an adaptive explicit Runge-Kutta scheme.

Conclusion: The IMEX approach offers a practical and efficient alternative for two-fluid plasma turbulence simulations, avoiding full implicit cost while overcoming explicit stability constraints.

Abstract: In this work, a globally stiffly accurate Implicit-Explicit (IMEX) Runge-Kutta scheme is developed and implemented in the GBS code [Ricci et al., Plasma Phys. Control. Fusion, 2012], for two-fluid plasma turbulence simulations. The stiffest phenomena, governed by shear Alfvén waves and parallel diffusion, are treated implicitly, while the remaining non-stiff terms are advanced explicitly. This splitting enables time steps well beyond the Courant-Friedrichs-Lewy limit, without incurring the full computational cost of a globally implicit formulation. To efficiently solve the implicit subsystem at each time step, a three-dimensional physics-based preconditioner, inspired by techniques developed in the magnetohydrodynamic (MHD) context, is used. The resulting framework is verified through the method of manufactured solutions and exhibits both algorithmic and parallel scalability. Significant advantages in numerical stability and computational efficiency are demonstrated with respect to an adaptive explicit Runge-Kutta scheme.

</details>


### [131] [A packet-level digital hardware twin for commissioning megahertz diagnostic edge AI and plasma control system integration in tokamaks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33994)
*Semin Joung,Abhilasha Dave,Luca Scomparin,Filipp Khabanov,Zheng Yan,Benedikt Geiger,George McKee,Ryan N. Coffee,David R. Smith*

Main category: physics.plasm-ph

TL;DR: A packet-level digital hardware twin simulates a 64-channel 1 MHz BES diagnostic within a 96-channel dual-carrier acquisition system, modeling the full path from UDP transport through edge inference to a PCS-like control cycle, enabling reproducible latency and fail-safe testing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: High-bandwidth plasma diagnostics feed real-time tokamak control algorithms, but commissioning the full path from diagnostic sampling to control-system handoff is hard due to extreme sampling rates. Existing emulation typically operates only at the array level and misses transport details.

Method: The simulator generates actual binary UDP payloads and PCAP files at packet level. It represents a 64-channel 1 MHz BES diagnostic in a 96-channel dual-carrier system (two 48-channel streams with SPAD0 counters), 10 GbE hardware UDP transport, packet loss/jitter, FPGA parsing and dual-carrier alignment, causal preprocessing, edge inference, compact Ethernet result packets, receiver-side shared state, and a 1 kHz PCS-like control cycle.

Result: Baseline of 20 samples/packet yields 50,000 packets/s and 100 MB/s per carrier. A 110 ms run produces 11,000 HUDP packets. An intentionally dropped packet is caught by sample-counter continuity logic, invalidating overlapping 128-sample windows without silent interpolation. Valid windows show median last-input-to-shared-memory latency of 91.6 us (99th percentile 108.1 us). An OS loopback test preserves sequence and CRC for 20/20 packets.

Conclusion: The framework offers a reproducible environment for testing diagnostic-to-accelerator interfaces and fail-safe behavior, with interactive GUI exposing timing, packetization, faults, inference thresholds, and control-state inspection for deployment on fusion devices.

Abstract: High-bandwidth plasma diagnostics increasingly provide inputs to machine learning and signal-processing algorithms intended for real-time tokamak control, but the complete path from diagnostic sampling to control-system handoff is difficult to commission because of their sampling rates. We develop a packet-level digital hardware twin for the megahertz diagnostic edge-AI architecture. The simulator represents a 64-channel, 1 MHz beam emission spectroscopy (BES) diagnostic embedded in a 96-channel dual-carrier acquisition system, two 48-channel streams with SPAD0 sample counters, 10 GbE Hardware UDP transport, packet loss and network jitter, FPGA parsing and dual-carrier alignment, causal preprocessing and edge inference, a compact Ethernet result packet, receiver-side shared state, and a 1 kHz PCS-like control cycle. Binary UDP payloads and PCAP files are generated rather than emulating transport only at the array level. With a baseline of 20 samples per packet, each carrier generates 50,000 packets s$^{-1}$ and 100 MB s$^{-1}$ of user payload. A 110 ms reference run produces 11,000 HUDP packets; an intentionally dropped 20-sample packet is detected by the sample-counter continuity logic and invalidates the two overlapping 128-sample inference windows without silent interpolation. For valid windows, the configured engineering latency model gives a median last-input-to-shared-memory latency of 91.6 us and a 99th percentile of 108.1 us. A separate operating-system loopback test sends binary FPGA-result datagrams through a UDP receiver into POSIX shared memory and preserves packet sequence and CRC for 20/20 packets. Interactive GUI interfaces expose timing, packetization, network faults, inference thresholds, and control-state inspection. The framework provides a reproducible environment for testing diagnostic-to-accelerator interfaces and fail-safe behavior for deployment on fusion devices.

</details>


### [132] [Observer-Based Model Predictive Control for Isoflux Regulation in the EXL-50U Spherical Tokamak](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34076)
*Chang Lu,Jiayi Zhi,Xian Guo,Tianyuan Liu*

Main category: physics.plasm-ph

TL;DR: Develops observer-based constrained MPC for isoflux control on the EXL-50U spherical tokamak, achieving joint plasma-current and boundary-flux regulation with sub-millisecond computation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Isoflux control in spherical tokamaks demands coordinated plasma-current and boundary regulation under coil-voltage and computational constraints, motivating a control approach that handles constraints while remaining computationally feasible for real-time operation.

Method: Vacuum-vessel spatial coarsening reduces a 538-state linearized model to 40 states. A Kalman observer supplies full-state estimates, and MPC jointly optimizes current and boundary-flux tracking with explicit voltage bounds. Matrix precomputation, specialized solver code generation, and structured linear-system solution cut online computation. Evaluated via nonlinear free-boundary Grad-Shafranov evolutive simulations against PID and LQR.

Result: Nominal MPC RMS errors: 1.126 kA (plasma current), 0.0164 m (LCFS geometry), 2.463 mWb (worst flux channel), all below baselines. Under noise boundary error is 0.0199 m with sustained regulation. MPC completes a limiter-to-divertor transition without relinearization/retuning and maintains regulation under delay and combined disturbances. Mean computation times 0.413-0.441 ms with all 99th percentiles below 1 ms.

Conclusion: Results support joint current and isoflux regulation and millisecond-scale computational feasibility, providing a basis for future EXL-50U experiments.

Abstract: Isoflux control in spherical tokamaks requires coordinated plasma-current and boundary regulation under coil-voltage and computational constraints. This study develops observer-based constrained model predictive control (MPC) for EXL-50U. Vacuum-vessel spatial coarsening reduces a 538-state linearized model to 40 states while retaining control-relevant responses. A Kalman observer provides full-state estimates, and MPC jointly optimizes current and boundary-flux tracking with explicit voltage bounds. Matrix precomputation, specialized solver code generation, and structured linear-system solution reduce online computation.
  Nonlinear free-boundary Grad--Shafranov evolutive simulations evaluate nominal operation, measurement noise, configuration mismatch, a one-step delay, and combined noise and delay against proportional--integral--derivative (PID) control and a linear--quadratic regulator (LQR). Nominal MPC root-mean-square errors are 1.126 kA for plasma current, 0.0164 m for last-closed-flux-surface geometry, and 2.463 mWb for the worst flux channel, all below both baselines. With noise, the boundary error is 0.0199 m over the common evaluation window, and regulation is sustained throughout the test. MPC completes a limiter-to-divertor transition without relinearization or retuning and maintains regulation under delay and combined disturbances. Across all five cases, mean MPC computation times are 0.413--0.441 ms, with all 99th percentiles below 1 ms. These results support joint current and isoflux regulation and millisecond-scale computational feasibility, providing a basis for future EXL-50U experiments.

</details>


### [133] [Electrons with Anomalous Energy Generated in Vacuum Diodes with Different Pulse Duration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34350)
*Vasily Kozhevnikov,Victor Tarasenko,Maxim Vorobyov,Evgenii Baksht,Andrey Kozyrev*

Main category: physics.plasm-ph

TL;DR: This experimental study shows that anomalous energy electrons (AEE) with kinetic energy exceeding eU are generated in vacuum diodes only under nanosecond or sub-nanosecond voltage pulses, with AEE fractions up to 25%, using an AI-based method to reconstruct electron energy spectra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the conditions under which anomalous energy electrons (AEE) appear in vacuum diodes, since electrons with kinetic energy T > eU are physically unexpected, and to rule out bremsstrahlung photoelectric absorption as their source.

Method: Two experimental setups generated electron beams with different pulse durations (varying amplitude and pulse-duration conditions). Electron energy spectra were reconstructed from beam attenuation curves using an AI-driven methodology that solves an ill-posed inverse problem for the Fredholm equation.

Result: Vacuum diodes generate electron beams with a significant AEE fraction (up to 25%) only when operating with nanosecond-long voltage pulses. AEE generation is confirmed for nanosecond- or sub-nanosecond voltage pulses. Photoelectric absorption of bremsstrahlung cannot produce significant AEE in vacuum discharges.

Conclusion: AEE in vacuum diodes require short (nanosecond or sub-nanosecond) voltage pulses, and bremsstrahlung photoelectric absorption is ruled out as their cause.

Abstract: This paper presents results from an experimental study in which anomalous energy electrons (AEE) appear in vacuum diodes under varying amplitude and pulse-duration conditions. AEE refer to high-energy discharge electrons with kinetic energy $T$ exceeding $eU$ values (where $e$ is the electron charge and $U$ is the amplitude gap voltage). Here, two experimental setups were used to generate electron beams with different pulse durations. The electron energy spectra were reconstructed from the beam attenuation curves using an AI-driven methodology for solving an ill-posed inverse problem for the Fredholm equation. This confirms that vacuum diodes generate electron beams with a significant AEE fraction (up to 25~\%) only when operating with nanosecond-long voltage pulses. It has been confirmed that AEE in vacuum is generated using nanosecond- or sub-nanosecond voltage pulses. Experiments also indicate that photoelectric absorption of bremsstrahlung cannot produce significant AEE in vacuum discharges.

</details>


<div id='math.DG'></div>

# math.DG [[Back]](#toc)

### [134] [On the degenerate Weyl problem on isometric immersions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28905)
*Siran Li,Xiangxiang Su*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: The paper solves degenerate Weyl problems for isometric embeddings/immersions of $S^2$ under nonnegative Gaussian curvature conditions, establishing intrinsic $C^{2,1}$ embeddings into $\mathbb{R}^3$ and $W^{3,p}$ immersions into general three-manifolds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The classical Weyl problem concerns isometric embeddings of two-spheres into $\mathbb{R}^3$ or general three-manifolds, typically assuming strictly positive Gaussian curvature. Extending this to the degenerate case where curvature is only nonnegative is difficult and previously lacked purely intrinsic conditions.

Method: The authors address the degenerate Weyl problem (nonnegative Gaussian curvature $K_g \geq 0$) via intrinsic metric conditions: positive-definite Hessian at finitely many zero-curvature points, and integral/pinching hypotheses such as $(K_g-K_0)^{-1/2}\in L^p$ combined with conditions on approximate nondegenerate immersions.

Result: First, a smooth metric on $S^2$ with strictly positive curvature except at finitely many points with positive-definite Hessian admits a global $C^{2,1}$-isometric embedding into $\mathbb{R}^3$. Second, for a simply-connected ambient three-manifold with $K_g \geq K_0 \geq \mathrm{sec}_{\bar g}$, $(K_g-K_0)^{-1/2}\in L^p$ ($p\geq2$), and a pinching condition, a $W^{3,p}$-isometric immersion exists. The nondegenerate Weyl problem into general simply-connected ambient three-manifolds is also resolved for $C^{2,1}$ metrics.

Conclusion: The work provides the first results on the degenerate Weyl problem using purely intrinsic conditions on the metric, and extends the nondegenerate Weyl problem to general simply-connected ambient three-manifolds for $C^{2,1}$ data.

Abstract: This paper is concerned with the Weyl problem, \emph{i.e.}, the existence of isometric immersions or embeddings of two-spheres into the three-dimensional Euclidean space or general ambient three-manifolds. We establish two results on the degenerate Weyl problem, namely when the Gaussian curvature $K_g$ is only nonnegative rather than strictly positive. First, for a smooth metric $g$ on the two-sphere, if $K_g$ is strictly positive except at finitely many points where the Hessian of $K_g$ is positive definite, then $g$ admits a global $C^{2,1}$-isometric embedding into $\mathbb{R}^3$. It appears to be the first result on the degenerate Weyl problem with purely intrinsic conditions on $g$. Second, for a general simply-connected ambient three-manifold $(\mathcal{M},{\overline{g}})$, if $K_g \geq K_0 \geq {\rm sec}_{\overline{g}}$ for some constant $K_0$, $(K_g-K_0)^{-1/2} \in L^p$ with $p \geq 2$, and a certain uniform pinching condition holds for approximate nondegenerate isometric immersions, then there exists a $W^{3,p}$-isometric immersion. Alongside we also resolve the nondegenerate Weyl problem (\emph{i.e.}, when $K_g>0$) into general simply-connected ambient three-manifolds for $g$, ${\overline{g}} \in C^{2,1}$.

</details>


### [135] [Energy convexity and uniformity of the $H$-surface flow in $\mathbb{R}^{3}$ with Dirichlet boundary condition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30740)
*Da Rong Cheng,Longzhi Lin,Xin Zhou*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: The paper proves a convexity property for the energy functional of prescribed-mean-curvature surfaces, and uses it to establish long-time convergence of the H-surface flow to a unique stationary solution for small-energy initial data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding the energy landscape of prescribed-mean-curvature surfaces and the long-time behavior of their gradient flows is a natural and important problem in geometric analysis and PDE. Convexity of the energy near small-energy configurations would give strong control over the flow and its limits.

Method: First, prove convexity of the energy functional restricted to maps from the unit 2-disk with small Dirichlet energy and fixed boundary value, assuming the 3-form H·vol has a primitive with suitable bounds. Second, under milder assumptions on H, establish an analogous convexity estimate along the H-surface heat flow for small initial Dirichlet energy—first for classical solutions, then extended to weak solutions via approximation using a quantitative uniqueness result adapted from harmonic map heat flow. Finally, derive uniform convergence to a unique limit solving the H-surface system.

Result: A convexity property for the energy functional and an analogous convexity estimate along the H-surface flow; a quantitative uniqueness result extended by approximation to weak solutions; and uniform convergence at infinite time of the H-surface flow (small-energy C^0 ∩ W^{1,2} initial data on the unit 2-disk) to a unique limit solving the stationary H-surface system with the same boundary value.

Conclusion: The convexity estimates provide a robust framework for studying prescribed-mean-curvature energy and its heat flow, yielding uniqueness and long-time convergence results for small-energy data, thereby deepening the understanding of the H-surface flow's asymptotic behavior.

Abstract: We prove that the energy functional associated with surfaces of prescribed mean curvature in $\mathbb{R}^3$ exhibits a convexity property when restricted to maps from the unit $2$-disk having small Dirichlet energy and a fixed boundary value, provided that the $3$-form $H \cdot \text{vol}_{\mathbb{R}^3}$ has a primitive satisfying certain bounds.
  Under milder assumptions on $H:\mathbb{R}^3 \to \mathbb{R}$, we show that an analogous convexity estimate holds along the heat flow of the functional (the $H$-surface flow) when the initial Dirichlet energy is sufficiently small. This is done first for classical solutions, and then extended by approximation to weak solutions using a quantitative uniqueness result adapted from previous work on the harmonic map heat flow. As a consequence of the convexity estimate, we show that the $H$-surface flow with small-energy initial map of class $C^0 \cap W^{1, 2}$ on the unit $2$-disk converges uniformly to a unique limit at infinite time, which solves the corresponding stationary problem (the $H$-surface system) with the same boundary value.

</details>


### [136] [Genus, Morse Index, and Area of Minimal Surfaces in Three-Manifolds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32001)
*Riccardo Caniato*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: This paper proves a genus-index-area bound for minimal surfaces in closed 3-manifolds, resolving Song's conjecture, and derives explicit genus and index bounds under curvature assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Song conjectured an additive genus-index-area estimate for minimal surfaces in closed Riemannian three-manifolds. The goal is to establish this bound without orientability or two-sidedness assumptions and to derive curvature-dependent genus bounds.

Method: The authors work in closed Riemannian three-manifolds $(M^3,\bar g)$ and prove existence of a constant $C>0$ bounding the first $\mathbb{Z}_2$-Betti number of any closed smoothly embedded minimal surface via its index and area. For the orientable two-sided minimal immersion case, they derive explicit genus bounds under a lower sectional curvature bound, and prove a universal index bound under positive Ricci curvature.

Result: Every closed smoothly embedded minimal surface $\Sigma\subset M$ satisfies $b_1(\Sigma;\mathbb{Z}_2)\le C(\operatorname{Ind}(\Sigma)+\operatorname{Area}(\Sigma))$. For closed connected orientable two-sided minimal immersions, explicit genus bounds hold under a lower ambient sectional curvature bound. Under positive Ricci curvature, $\gamma(\Sigma)\le 8\operatorname{Ind}(\Sigma)$.

Conclusion: The additive genus-index-area estimate conjectured by Song is established in full generality, along with explicit curvature-dependent genus bounds and a universal index-genus inequality under positive Ricci curvature.

Abstract: We prove that, in every closed Riemannian three-manifold $(M^3,\bar g)$, there exists a constant $C>0$ such that every closed smoothly embedded minimal surface $Σ\subset M$ satisfies $b_1(Σ;\mathbb{Z}_2)\le C\bigl(\operatorname{Ind}(Σ)+\operatorname{Area}(Σ)\bigr)$. The estimate holds without orientability or two-sidedness assumptions and establishes the additive genus-index-area estimate conjectured by Song. For closed connected orientable two-sided minimal immersions, we also obtain explicit genus bounds under a lower bound on the ambient sectional curvature. Under positive ambient Ricci curvature, we prove the universal inequality $γ(Σ)\le 8\operatorname{Ind}(Σ)$.

</details>


### [137] [Alternative proof of the gradient estimate for complex Hessian equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33091)
*Jianchun Chu,Yaxiong Liu*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: An alternative proof of the gradient estimate for complex Hessian equations on compact Kähler manifolds, avoiding the blow-up argument, with clarified dependence on the RHS, extended to the boundary case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing gradient estimates for complex Hessian equations on compact Kähler manifolds rely on a blow-up argument, and the precise dependence on the right-hand side is not clearly understood. The authors seek a more direct approach and clarity on this dependence.

Method: A direct approach avoiding the blow-up argument, providing an alternative proof of the gradient estimate; the same technique is adapted to the setting of Kähler manifolds with boundary.

Result: A new proof of the gradient estimate and an explicit description of its dependence on the right-hand side; analogous results obtained for Kähler manifolds with boundary.

Conclusion: The gradient estimate for complex Hessian equations can be derived without blow-up techniques, yielding clearer dependence on the RHS and extending to the boundary case.

Abstract: For complex Hessian equations on compact Kähler manifolds, we give an alternative proof of the gradient estimate without using blow-up argument, and clarify its dependence on the right-hand side. In addition, using the similar approach, the case of Kähler manifolds with boundary is also investigated.

</details>


### [138] [Isoperimetric bounds for the weighted Neumann and Steklov eigenvalues in dimensions three through six](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33555)
*Daguang Chen,Hao Liu,Chengxi Yang*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: The paper establishes sharp isoperimetric bounds for the mass-normalized first weighted Neumann eigenvalue on bounded Euclidean domains in dimensions 3-6, and proves a strict sharp Steklov bound, resolving a question by Vinokurov.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Weighted Neumann eigenvalues and their isoperimetric bounds are central in spectral geometry, and a sharp bound for the mass-normalized first weighted Neumann eigenvalue was previously unknown; Vinokurov posed a specific question (Question 1.12) about this.

Method: The authors prove an upper bound for the first weighted Neumann eigenvalue over all finite nonnegative nonatomic measures on the domain closure, expressed via a constant involving the unit ball volume and an angle from the first stationary radius of the regular rotational harmonic-map profile; they also derive a strict Steklov-type bound for admissible domains with finite boundary measure.

Result: For 3 ≤ n ≤ 6, the sharp bound is attained on balls by a smooth positive radial density. A strict sharp Steklov bound is also proved, with the constant approached by C^1 perforated domains.

Conclusion: The sharp isoperimetric bound for the mass-normalized first weighted Neumann eigenvalue is determined in dimensions 3-6, answering Vinokurov's Question 1.12 affirmatively, and a related strict sharp Steklov bound is established.

Abstract: For $3\le n\le6$, we determine the sharp isoperimetric bound for the mass-normalized first weighted Neumann eigenvalue on bounded Euclidean domains. For every finite nonnegative nonatomic measure $μ$ on $\overlineΩ$,
  \begin{equation*}
  \overlineλ_1^N(Ω,μ)\le\frac{n(n-1)}{n-2}ω_n\sin^2\vartheta_n\left(\frac{|Ω|}{ω_n}\right)^{(n-2)/n},
  \end{equation*}
  where $ω_n$ is the volume of the unit ball and $\vartheta_n\in(π/2,π)$ is the angle at the first stationary radius of the regular rotational harmonic-map profile. The bound is attained on balls by a smooth positive radial density. In fact, we affirmatively resolve Question~1.12 posed by Vinokurov \cite{Vinokurov2026}. Furthermore, for admissible domains with finite boundary measure, we also prove the sharp strict Steklov bound
  \begin{equation*}
  σ_1(Ω)|\partialΩ|\,|Ω|^{(2-n)/n}<\frac{n(n-1)}{n-2}ω_n^{2/n}\sin^2\vartheta_n,
  \end{equation*}
  whose constant is approached by $C^1$ perforated domains.

</details>


### [139] [The Asymptotic Plateau Problem for $p$-Convex Hypersurfaces in Hyperbolic Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34751)
*Shujun Shi,Zhenan Sui*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: The paper solves the asymptotic Plateau problem in hyperbolic space for curvature given by the normalized geometric mean of p-fold sums of principal curvatures, proving existence and uniqueness of complete admissible vertical graphs for a wide range of dimensions and parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior results only covered the special cases (n,p)=(3,2) and (4,3). The authors aim to extend existence and uniqueness of complete admissible vertical graphs to all n≥3 and 2≤p≤n-1, addressing a broader class of curvature functions in the asymptotic Plateau problem.

Method: The proof relies on a uniform curvature estimate with respect to the positive boundary height in approximating Dirichlet problems. It establishes two lower bounds for the full third-derivative quadratic form under the linearized constraints: a sharp universal bound with coefficient 2/p and a stronger direction-dependent bound when the relevant principal curvature lies in a specified range.

Result: For every n≥3, 2≤p≤n-1, and σ∈(0,1), each bounded domain Ω⊂ℝⁿ with smooth mean-convex boundary admits a unique complete admissible vertical graph with prescribed curvature σ and asymptotic boundary ∂Ω×{0}.

Conclusion: The result extends known cases (n,p)=(3,2) and (4,3) to the full range n≥3, 2≤p≤n-1, establishing existence and uniqueness via uniform curvature estimates and refined lower bounds on the third-derivative quadratic form.

Abstract: We solve the asymptotic Plateau problem in hyperbolic space for the curvature given by the normalized geometric mean of the $p$-fold sums of the principal curvatures. For every $n\ge3$, $2\le p\le n-1$, and $σ\in(0,1)$, each bounded domain $Ω\subset\mathbb{R}^n$ with smooth mean-convex boundary admits a unique complete admissible vertical graph with prescribed curvature $σ$ and asymptotic boundary $\partialΩ\times\{0\}$. The result extends the known cases $(n,p)=(3,2)$ and $(4,3)$ to the stated range. The key curvature estimate is uniform with respect to the positive boundary height in the approximating Dirichlet problems. The proof establishes two lower bounds for the full third-derivative quadratic form under the linearized constraints: a sharp universal bound with coefficient $2/p$ and a stronger direction-dependent bound when the relevant principal curvature lies in a specified range.

</details>


### [140] [Obata theorem of $σ_k$-flat and constant associated $k$th boundary curvature equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34835)
*Xuezhang Chen,Wei Wei*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: The paper proves an Obata-type theorem on the upper hemisphere for a σ_k-flat metric with positive constant boundary B_k-curvature, using the Obata-Escobar argument. This concerns the equation initiated by Case-Wang (Adv. Math. 337, 2018).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper is motivated by the boundary σ_k-curvature equation initiated by Case-Wang (Adv. Math. 337, 2018), aiming to extend Obata-type rigidity results to σ_k-flat metrics with constant boundary B_k-curvature on the upper hemisphere.

Method: It uses the Obata-Escobar argument applied on the upper hemisphere to establish the Obata theorem for the σ_k-flat and positive constant boundary B_k-curvature equation.

Result: An Obata theorem is established for σ_k-flat metrics with positive constant boundary B_k-curvature on the upper hemisphere.

Conclusion: The σ_k-flat manifold with positive constant boundary B_k-curvature on the upper hemisphere is rigid, characterized via the Obata-Escobar argument, extending Case-Wang's framework.

Abstract: On the upper hemisphere, we use the Obata-Escobar argument to establish an Obata theorem for $σ_k$-flat and positive constant boundary $\mathcal B_k$-curvature equation, which was initiated by Case-Wang [Adv. Math. 337 (2018), 83-106].

</details>


### [141] [Another sharp criterion for Dirac zero modes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34958)
*Guofang Wang,Mingwei Zhang*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: This paper resolves a Frank–Loss open problem by proving a sharp lower bound on ‖dA♭‖_{n/2} for nontrivial Dirac zero modes on S^n (n≥3, p>n/(n−1)), with equality attainable iff n is odd.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Frank and Loss posed an open problem concerning Dirac zero modes on the sphere; the n=3 case was solved earlier by the authors by a different method, and the remaining cases required new approaches.

Method: The proof splits into three cases—odd n≥5, even n≥5, and n=4—each needing distinct techniques. For n≥5, the argument reduces to an improved Sobolev inequality on S^n under a barycenter constraint, yielding a universal estimate for 𝔞_n.

Result: The sharp bound ‖dA♭‖_{n/2} ≥ [n/2]^{−1/2} n(n−1)/2 · ω_n^{2/n} is established, with equality iff n is odd; in that case φ is a Killing spinor and A is a real multiple of the associated Reeb field, modulo conformal and gauge transformations.

Conclusion: The Frank–Loss problem is resolved for all n≥3. The exact value of the Sobolev constant 𝔞_n remains an open question.

Abstract: We resolve an open problem posed by Frank--Loss. Let $n\ge 3$ and let $\varphi\in L^p(\mathbb{S}^n)$, with $\frac{n}{n-1}<p<\infty$, be a nontrivial Dirac zero mode on $\mathbb{S}^n$, i.e. a nonzero spinor satisfying \begin{equation*}
  D\varphi = iA\cdot\varphi, \end{equation*} where $D$ is the Dirac operator and $A$ is a vector field with $\mathrm{d} A^\flat\in L^{n/2}$. We prove the sharp lower bound \begin{equation*}
  \|\mathrm{d} A^\flat\|_{\frac{n}{2}} \ge 2\left[\frac{n}{2}\right]^{-\frac12}\frac{n-1}{n-2}S_n
  = \left[\frac{n}{2}\right]^{-\frac12}\frac{n(n-1)}{2}ω_n^{\frac{2}{n}}. \end{equation*} Equality is attainable if and only if $n$ is odd; in that case, modulo conformal and gauge transformations, $\varphi$ is a Killing spinor and $A$ is a real multiple of the Reeb field associated with $\varphi$. The case $n=3$ was proved in our recent paper using a different method. In the paper we divide the remaining cases into 3 cases: i) $n\ge 5$ is odd, ii) $n\ge 5$ is even and iii) $n=4$. All these cases need to use different methods.
  For $n\ge 5$, the argument crucially reduces to an improved Sobolev inequality on $\mathbb{S}^n$ under a barycenter constraint. Specifically, for $u\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb{S}^n)$ we consider \begin{equation*}
  \mathfrak{a}_n := \inf\Bigg\{ \frac{ \int \Big(|\nabla u|^2 + \frac{n(n-2)}{4}u^2\Big) - \frac{n(n-2)}{4}ω_n^{\frac{2}{n}}\|u\|_{\frac{2n}{n-2}}^2 }{ ω_n^{\frac{2}{n}}\|u\|_{\frac{2n}{n-2}}^2 - \int u^2 } \,\Bigg|\, \int x|u|^{\frac{2n}{n-2}}=0,\ ω_n^{\frac{2}{n}}\|u\|_{\frac{2n}{n-2}}^2 - \int u^2>0 \Bigg\}, \end{equation*} and we obtain the following universal estimate \begin{equation*}
  \mathfrak{a}_n > \frac{n(n-2)}{4(n^2-3n+1)}, \end{equation*} which is enough for our aim. Determining the exact value of $\mathfrak{a}_n$ remains open.

</details>


### [142] [Supercritical dual k-Minkowski flows with general prescribed data: a priori estimates and asymptotic convergence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35241)
*Hongyi Sheng,Weimin Sheng,Jiazhuo Yang*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: For the normalized contracting flow X_t = -f(ν)|X|^α σ_k(κ)ν + βX with β = C_n^k, in the supercritical range α > k+1, the flow exists for all time and converges exponentially in C^∞ from any smooth strictly convex initial hypersurface enclosing the origin to the unique strictly convex solution of f(x)r^α σ_k(κ) = βu.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existence of a strictly convex solution of the stationary equation for arbitrary positive angular data was previously obtained by Bryan-Ivaki-Scheuer via an expanding-type flow from a barrier. However, convergence of the normalized contracting flow from arbitrary initial data, with an explicit exponential rate, and uniqueness of the limit, remained open.

Method: Study the normalized contracting flow X_t = -f(ν)|X|^α σ_k(κ)ν + βX, β = C_n^k, driven by the relative residual p = (log u)_t = β - f(x)r^α σ_k(κ)/u. Show the L^∞ norm of p is nonincreasing and decays with explicit exponent β(α-k-1), proving residual estimates before and independently of curvature estimates.

Result: The flow exists for all time and converges exponentially in C^∞ from every smooth strictly convex initial hypersurface enclosing the origin to the unique smooth strictly convex solution of the stationary equation. The convergence is driven by the relative residual whose L^∞ norm is nonincreasing and decays with explicit exponent β(α-k-1).

Conclusion: The paper establishes convergence of the normalized contracting flow from arbitrary initial data in the supercritical range α > k+1, with an explicit exponential rate, together with uniqueness of the limit, complementing the earlier existence result of Bryan-Ivaki-Scheuer.

Abstract: Let k be an integer from 1 to n, let f be a positive smooth function on the unit sphere, and consider the normalized flow X_t = -f({nu})|X|^{alpha} sigma_k({kappa}) {nu} + beta X, beta = C_n^k. In the supercritical range alpha > k+1, we prove the flow exists for all time and converges exponentially in C^{infty}, from every smooth strictly convex initial hypersurface enclosing the origin, to the unique smooth strictly convex solution of f(x)r^{alpha} sigma_k({kappa}) = beta u. The convergence is driven by the relative residual p = (log u)_t = beta - f(x)r^{alpha} {sigma}_k({kappa})/u, whose L^{infty} norm is nonincreasing and decays with the explicit exponent beta(alpha-k-1); the residual estimates are proved before, and independently of, the curvature estimates. Existence of a strictly convex solution of the stationary equation in this range, for arbitrary positive angular data, was previously obtained by Bryan-Ivaki-Scheuer through an expanding-type flow started from a barrier; the contribution here is the convergence of the normalized contracting flow from arbitrary initial data, with an explicit exponential rate, together with uniqueness of the limit.

</details>


<div id='math.FA'></div>

# math.FA [[Back]](#toc)

### [143] [Bourgain--Brezis selection maps for Hodge systems in Besov and Triebel--Lizorkin spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33876)
*Diogo Arsénio*

Main category: math.FA

TL;DR: The paper establishes Bourgain–Brezis selection maps for Hodge systems in new ranges of Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin spaces, generalizing earlier results and highlighting the trace-free Beurling–Ahlfors transform's role.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the known range of solutions to the Bourgain–Brezis problem for Hodge systems and to understand the role of the trace-free Beurling–Ahlfors transform.

Method: Combines the trace-free Beurling–Ahlfors transform with a nonlinear duality method, using tools from Stolyarov (Maz'ya's Φ-inequalities) to control concentrations.

Result: Yields selection maps in Besov spaces Ḃ^{n/p}_{p,q}(R^n) for 1≤q≤p≤2, and in Triebel–Lizorkin spaces Ḟ^{n/p}_{p,q}(R^n) for 1<p≤q≤2, for every dimension n≥2 and form degree 1≤l≤n−1.

Conclusion: The results considerably extend the known range of solutions to the Bourgain–Brezis problem, overlapping with some earlier work, and underscore the importance of the trace-free Beurling–Ahlfors transform.

Abstract: We establish Bourgain--Brezis selection maps for Hodge systems in new ranges of Besov and Triebel--Lizorkin spaces. Our approach combines the trace-free Beurling--Ahlfors transform with a nonlinear duality method. To control concentrations, we use tools developed by Stolyarov in the study of Maz'ya's $Φ$-inequalities. These methods yield selection maps in Besov spaces $\dot B^{n/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^n)$ for every $1\leq q\leq p\leq 2$, and in Triebel--Lizorkin spaces $\dot F^{n/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^n)$ for every $1<p\leq q\leq 2$. The theorems hold for every dimension $n\geq 2$ and every form degree $1\leq l\leq n-1$. Although our results overlap with some earlier work, they considerably extend the known range of solutions to the Bourgain--Brezis problem and underscore the importance of the trace-free Beurling--Ahlfors transform in its resolution.

</details>


### [144] [Modulation spaces: from Feichtinger's original definition to modern Gabor and symplectic characterizations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33894)
*Antonio Caputo,Elena Cordero,Gianluca Giachi,Luigi Rodino*

Main category: math.FA

TL;DR: An expository survey of modulation spaces, tracing Feichtinger's local-global principle from locally compact abelian groups through symplectic and metaplectic formulations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and clarify the many equivalent descriptions and extensions of modulation spaces, and to situate recent symplectic/metaplectic theories within Feichtinger's original framework.

Method: Traces a common local-global principle across bounded uniform partitions of unity, the short-time Fourier transform, coorbit methods, and Gabor coefficients; distinguishes equivalent descriptions from weighted, quasi-Banach, and Gelfand-Shilov extensions; analyzes metaplectic Wigner distributions via shift-invertibility.

Result: In the shift-invertible case, metaplectic Wigner distributions reduce (up to chirps and linear phase-space changes) to rescaled short-time Fourier transforms; also covers Gabor matrices, convolution and embedding estimates, and an application to a nonlinear dispersive equation.

Conclusion: The structural reduction explains how metaplectic Wigner distributions characterize modulation and Wiener amalgam spaces and enables discretization by metaplectic Gabor frames, placing symplectic theory within Feichtinger's modulation-space framework.

Abstract: We present an expository excursus on modulation spaces, from Feichtinger's original construction on locally compact abelian groups to recent symplectic formulations. We trace the same local-global principle through bounded uniform partitions of unity, the short-time Fourier transform, coorbit methods, and Gabor coefficients, while distinguishing equivalent descriptions from the weighted, quasi-Banach, and Gelfand-Shilov extensions of the underlying functional setting. We also discuss Gabor matrices, convolution and embedding estimates, and an application to a nonlinear dispersive equation. The final part is devoted to metaplectic Wigner distributions $W_{\mathcal A}$. In the shift-invertible case these representations are, up to chirps and a linear change of phase-space variables, rescaled short-time Fourier transforms. This structural fact explains both their characterization of modulation and Wiener amalgam spaces and their discretization by metaplectic Gabor frames, and places the recent symplectic theory naturally within Feichtinger's modulation-space framework.

</details>


<div id='math.OC'></div>

# math.OC [[Back]](#toc)

### [145] [Tight Convergence Bounds for the Classical Kaczmarz Method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32034)
*Runbo Yu,Jelena Diakonikolas*

Main category: math.OC

TL;DR: This paper provides a tight characterization of the convergence of the classical (cyclic-update) Kaczmarz method, deriving exact worst-case linear and sublinear bounds and interpretable relaxations in terms of matrix row norms, correlations, rank, and singular values.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The classical Kaczmarz method (1937) is widely used, especially for inverse problems, but its precise convergence characterization has long been considered difficult. Existing bounds fail to explain the efficient convergence observed in practice for the cyclic-update method.

Method: The authors derive tight worst-case convergence bounds (both linear and sublinear) for classical Kaczmarz, expressed via a fixed matrix depending only on A. They then provide relaxations of these bounds in terms of row norms, row correlations, rank, and extremal positive singular values.

Result: The tight bounds are exactly attained by some problem instances. The relaxed bounds explain one-cycle convergence when rows are parallel or orthogonal, and their parameter dependence is shown to be necessary and near-optimal within a small constant factor.

Conclusion: The new bounds explain why classical cyclic Kaczmarz outperforms the randomized version when matrix rows are weakly correlated—commonly observed in inverse problems—providing theoretical justification for practical behavior.

Abstract: The classical method of Kaczmarz, introduced in 1937, is a textbook iterative method for solving linear systems $A x = b.$ Despite its widespread use, particularly in the context of solving inverse problems where it is commonly included in software packages within Matlab, Python, and Julia, the precise characterization of convergence has long been deemed difficult to obtain. While different bounds on convergence rates have been established, they are largely unsatisfying as they cannot explain the classical, cyclic-update method's efficient convergence in practice. In this work, we obtain a tight characterization of convergence of the classical Kaczmarz method, establishing both linear and sublinear convergence bounds. The worst-case tight (i.e., exactly attained by some instances in the considered family) bounds are expressed in terms of a fixed matrix that depends only on $A,$ but are not fully interpretable in terms of the matrix spectrum and row correlations, which had been observed to have an impact on convergence. We thus provide relaxations of these bounds that are fully expressible in terms of matrix row norms, row correlations, rank, and extremal positive singular values. The provided relaxed bounds explain one-cycle convergence in special cases where the matrix rows are all either parallel or orthogonal to each other. We further argue that the dependence on different parameters appearing in the bounds is necessary and within a small constant factor of the best attainable in the worst case. Finally, our bounds explain why the classical cyclic update is faster than the randomized one when matrix rows are weakly correlated, which is often observed in inverse problems where the cyclic method is used.

</details>


### [146] [Mixed-Noise Plug-and-Play with Infimal Convolution Fidelities and Multiple Priors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33897)
*Ziqi Qin,Hong Ye Tan,Ander Biguri,Jingwei Liang,Carola-Bibiane Schönlieb*

Main category: math.OC

TL;DR: This paper develops provably convergent Plug-and-Play (PnP) methods for mixed-noise inverse imaging using infimal-convolution fidelity, and extends PnP to multiple prior terms via Davis-Yin three-operator splitting.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: PnP algorithms combine a fidelity term with a pretrained denoiser to handle severe corruptions. However, existing methods typically assume a single noise type and a single prior, limiting their ability to handle mixed-noise forward processes and to preserve a valid Bayesian MAP interpretation.

Method: The authors use infimal convolution as a fidelity term for mixed-noise forward processes, yielding a joint MAP estimator over both noises while retaining the Bayesian MAP interpretation. They also extend PnP to multiple prior terms using Davis-Yin three-operator splitting, which can be paired with standard or mixed-noise infimal-convolution fidelities. Convergence is established under standard Kurdyka-Lojasiewicz conditions.

Result: Experiments on Laplace-Gaussian and Poisson-Gaussian noise show stable convergence of single- and multiple-prior PnP methods, while fidelity mismatch causes divergence. Infimal-convolution fidelity PnP scales to noise up to 38% standard deviation, and multiple priors reach different stationary points that qualitatively preserve more textural properties.

Conclusion: The proposed framework provides convergent PnP methods for mixed-noise imaging, with a principled Bayesian interpretation, multi-prior extensibility, robust scaling to high noise levels, and improved preservation of textural detail.

Abstract: Plug-and-Play (PnP) algorithms are a class of iterative methods for inverse imaging. Within an optimization algorithm, they combine a flexible fidelity term, encoding the forward operator, and a pretrained image denoiser, in order to deal with more severe corruptions such as blurring or downsampling when reconstructing an image. This work studies provably convergent PnP methods for mixed-noise forward processes by using the infimal convolution as a fidelity term, providing a statistical interpretation as a joint maximum a-posteriori estimator over both noises, and preserving the Bayesian MAP interpretation of PnP methods. Independently, we extend the PnP formulation to multiple prior terms using the Davis--Yin three-operator splitting. This extension can be combined with either standard fidelities or the proposed mixed-noise infimal-convolution fidelities. We verify that these generalized PnP methods are convergent under standard Kurdyka--Lojasiewicz conditions. Numerical experiments on Laplace-Gaussian and Poisson--Gaussian noise demonstrate stable convergence of single-prior and multiple-prior PnP methods, and divergence under fidelity mismatch. Furthermore, PnP with infimal convolution fidelities are able to scale to noise up to 38% standard deviation, with multiple priors reaching different stationary points that qualitatively preserve more textural properties.

</details>


### [147] [Quantitative Stability, Coercivity and Uniqueness of Optimal Transport Plans](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35574)
*William Ford*

Main category: math.OC

TL;DR: Proves quantitative stability, coercivity, and uniqueness results for quadratic optimal transport under perturbation of marginals, assuming Ahlfors regularity with exponent > d-1.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Optimal transport plans and maps are known to be stable, but quantitative rates under perturbation of both marginals are lacking; especially uniqueness (Brenier) has no quantitative counterpart.

Method: Assume one measure satisfies upper Ahlfors regularity with exponent strictly greater than d-1; analyze quadratic transport plans/maps in Wasserstein distance on compactly supported measures.

Result: Quantitative stability of plans and maps; a coercivity theorem (any measure with similar marginals and cost is quantitatively close to optimal plans); a quantitative uniqueness theorem controlling the Wasserstein diameter of optimal plans; sharpness examples for all exponents.

Conclusion: "Almost uniqueness" of optimal plans is quantified by the source measure being "almost regular"; all estimate exponents are shown sharp.

Abstract: For probability measures on $\mathbb{R}^d$ supported in fixed compact sets, we prove that quadratic optimal transport plans are quantitatively stable in Wasserstein distance under perturbation of both marginal measures, assuming that one of the initial measures satisfies an upper Ahlfors regularity condition with exponent strictly greater than $d-1$. Under the same assumption, we also prove novel quantitative stability results for optimal transport maps.
  Furthermore, we prove a coercivity theorem which states that any probability measure on the product space must be quantitatively close to the set of optimal plans, if it has similar marginals and a similar transport cost to the optimal value.
  Finally, we prove a quantitative uniqueness theorem which acts as a quantitative counterpart to Brenier's theorem. The Wasserstein diameter of the set of optimal plans is controlled by the distance of one marginal measure to a regular measure for which uniqueness holds. In this way, "almost uniqueness" of optimal plans is quantified by the source measure being "almost regular".
  Examples are provided which prove that the exponents of all estimates are sharp.

</details>


### [148] [Sharp Observability Costs for Strongly Coupled Parabolic Systems with Vanishing Principal Coupling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35087)
*Xiaomin Zhu*

Main category: math.OC

TL;DR: The paper determines exact blow-up rates of observability costs for strongly coupled parabolic systems as the coupling parameter vanishes, showing rates depend on the algebraic structure of the observation pair.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding how observability degrades in strongly coupled parabolic systems when the coupling parameter tends to zero, which is a singular perturbation setting where standard observability results may fail.

Method: Asymptotic analysis of the optimal observability cost under the Kalman rank condition, observing the system through a fixed matrix B on a positive-measure space-time subset, with rates derived from the algebraic structure of (B, K).

Result: Exact blow-up rates are determined; different components and combinations of the state can have different rates, with full-state observability cost governed by the largest rate. The worst-case minimum L^infinity-norm of null controls exhibits the same asymptotic behavior.

Conclusion: The blow-up rates of observability cost and null controls are explicitly characterized by the algebraic structure of (B, K), providing a precise link between coupling strength and observability loss.

Abstract: We study the loss of observability for a class of strongly coupled parabolic systems as the coupling parameter tends to zero. The system is observed through a fixed matrix $B$ on a measurable subset of space-time with positive measure. Under the Kalman rank condition, we determine the exact blow-up rates of the optimal observability cost. Different components of the state, as well as different combinations of them, may have different blow-up rates, and these rates are determined by the algebraic structure of the pair $(B,K)$. The full-state observability cost is determined by the largest of these rates. We also show that the worst-case minimum $L^\infty$-norm of null controls has the same asymptotic behavior.

</details>
